**Status:** REFERENCE (living residue ledger — rows are appended and closed, never deleted or edited; undated filename on purpose, it is a stable cross-reference target like `DOCS-INDEX.md`) · **Ledger-opened:** 2026-09-02 (owner-ratified, replaces "spawn a follow-up brief" — CLAUDE.md §📌)

# Residue ledger — defects a brief left behind, one row each, promoted on pickup

**What this is.** When a brief is executed or triaged and something verified is still wrong, it goes
here as **one row**, not as a new `-FOLLOWUPS-` brief. A follow-up brief is written **only by the
session that picks the row up to execute it** — so every follow-up brief has an owner the moment it
exists. Measured 2026-09-02 (the drain): 27 of 77 open briefs were `-FOLLOWUPS-` files created at
execution time, and none of them had an owner, because "spawn a follow-up" creates the file before
anyone has decided to do the work.

**Row contract** (gate-backed — `docs-ledger` check 8 in `tests/dex-tests.js`):

| column | rule |
|---|---|
| key | **`YYYY-MM-DD-short-slug`** — the date the row was written plus a few words naming the defect (`2026-09-02-oxyii-acks-unparsed`). Lowercase, digits and hyphens. Unique **by construction**, not by allocation: it needs no lookup, no counter and no coordination, and it reads in a back-reference |
| logged | `YYYY-MM-DD` the row was written |
| source | where the residue came from, backticked. Usually a `*-BRIEF.md` that left it — must exist, and its **Status:** line must carry `**Residue:** <key>` (bidirectional, like `Superseded-by`). **A residue with no parent brief names its real origin instead**: a repo path (`capture-host/tools/find_unwired.py`), which must exist in the tree, or a `#NNNN` PR. Non-brief sources neither need nor can carry a back-reference |
| defect | what is wrong — a verified defect, not a wish; **no `\|` in the cell** (a pipe splits the row and blinds the gate — measured on DOCS-INDEX 2026-09-02) |
| evidence | the line / hash / count / command that shows it — prose is not evidence |
| state | `OPEN` · `→ \`<NAME>-BRIEF.md\`` (promoted to a brief at pickup — that brief must exist) · `fixed #NNNN` (closed by one PR, no brief needed) · `withdrawn <key>` (the defect turned out NOT TO EXIST — the key names the row that withdrew it, which must itself say so; owner ruling 2026-09-13, gate-enforced by `docs-ledger` check8i) |

A row is closed by changing **only** its state cell. Nothing else on a row is ever edited; a wrong
row gets a new row that says so.
⚠️ **One exemption, and it is self-limiting: repairing a POINTER.** A reference to another row's
identity is not a claim about the world — it is a pointer, and the 2026-09-02 rename broke every one of
them. Replacing a retired `R<n>` with a backticked key that names a real row PRESERVES the claim;
leaving it dangling alters it, because the row then points nowhere. `residue-ids` permits exactly that
substitution and nothing else, checkable from the two versions of the file alone with no rename map and
no author's assurance — and once no `R<n>` survives anywhere it can never fire again.
Open count: `grep -cE '^\| [0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}-[a-z0-9-]+ \|.*\| OPEN \|' briefs/RESIDUE.md`
(verified 2026-09-02: 20 matches against 20 open rows, and 0 non-row lines match).
⚠️ The obvious form — `grep -c '| OPEN |'` — **counts this instruction line too**, because the line
quoting the pattern contains the pattern. It read 17 against 16 open rows on 2026-09-02. Anchor on a
row, not on a substring that your own documentation matches.

🔴 **Why the key is a date-slug and not `R<n>` (changed 2026-09-02, after FIVE collisions in one day).**
The ledger opened with a sequential counter. Two branches then minted the same number five times —
`R11` twice, `R12`, `R13`, `R15` — and the last one happened **within the hour of the rule being
argued out, between the two sessions arguing it, each having run the prescribed pre-push check and each
having received a correct answer from it.** The diagnosis is not carelessness and not staleness:
`origin/main` **cannot contain an id claimed in an open branch**, so the check the scheme demanded could
not return the right answer. Closing that properly meant enumerating every open PR's diff over the
network, racing anyone who pushed meanwhile, enforced by nothing.

> **A globally-unique identifier allocated from local information has no correct procedure.** Not a slow
> one, not an expensive one — none. The fix is not a better check; it is an id that needs no allocation.

Briefs and changesets in this repo have always been dated-slug and **neither has ever collided**; the
ledger was the only artifact inventing an allocation problem. The old `R<n>` keys were renamed in place
by one migration — the single exception to "a row is never edited", and it is recorded here rather than
performed quietly. `R7` never existed: it was announced in a message, minted around, and never written.

⚠️ **Never name the nearest brief to fill the source cell.** A residue surfaced by *repairing an
instrument* has no parent brief — the two orphans `find_unwired.py` found on 2026-09-02, once it
stopped counting a comment as a consumer, descend from the tool fix and from nothing else. A false
source **passes** check 8 (which verifies existence and the back-reference, not responsibility) while
sending whoever picks the row up to a brief that never left the defect. That is the fabricated
authority this ledger exists to prevent, committed inside the ledger. Name the origin that is true.

**Not retro-applied.** The `-FOLLOWUPS-` briefs that already exist keep their files and headers; this
ledger starts empty except for the residue named in the 2026-09-02 drain headers.

The threshold is 99 %, so the margin is ~0.35 pp and it is not a safety margin — it is the width of a rounding artifact. ⚠ n=1 DEVICE on 1 night (6 fragments of one session), so this characterises the H10's stamp FORMAT, not a population; do not quote it as a corpus statistic. CONSEQUENCE for the ECGDex migration in `2026-09-04-drawn-axis-consumers-unmigrated`: adding `&& !deviceDrawn` to `ecgdex-dsp.js:4648`/`:4678` is SAFE on real H10 data (it would not fire) and reds exactly 5 ecgdex assertions, all of them planted-drift recoveries whose synthetic axis is `i * 1000` — a PERFECT 100 % uniform column that no real device produces. So the fixture re-cut is the real work and it is small: give the synthetic device column the same rounded-rate structure a real H10 has. But settle the threshold first — a rule that separates real from synthetic by 0.35 pp of rounding is measuring the stamp format, not the provenance | fixed #2212 |

| 2026-09-03-escape-blind-pipe-split | 2026-09-03 | `tests/dex-tests.js` | Markdown table cells are counted by splitting on a bare pipe, which does not understand the backslash escape — so an ESCAPED pipe inside a cell reads as a cell boundary. `docs-ledger` check8b does this at `tests/dex-tests.js:26692` (`line.split(chr124)`), and the consequence is that a residue row may never quote a regex, a shell alternation or any prose containing a pipe, however correctly escaped | Hit TWICE in one hour on 2026-09-03, from OPPOSITE directions, which is what makes it a class rather than a nit. (a) check8b rejected a residue row as "7 cells, want 6" because the evidence quoted an alternation regex; the escape was correct and the counter was not. I reworded rather than widen the gate. (b) the same escape-blind split in my own generated tools index called a CORRECTLY escaped row corrupt, which sent me looking for a bug in the escaping that was not there — while the REAL defect one line away was the reverse (escaping the pipe without first escaping the backslash), found by CodeQL as js/incomplete-sanitization and fixed in #2154. A fix in either is evidence about the other. The honest counter honours the escape (a pipe preceded by an odd number of backslashes is literal) or the gate must say plainly that pipes are unsupported in a cell | fixed #2165 |

| 2026-09-03-selftest-all-unbounded-fanout | 2026-09-03 | `tools/selftest-all.mjs` | `selftest-all` spawns EVERY tool at once — `Promise.all(files.map(run))` at `:195`, no concurrency cap — 84 node subprocesses on 24 cores, against a fixed `TOOL_TIMEOUT_MS = 120000` at `:96`. A tool that needs real CPU is starved by its 83 siblings and killed at the cap, so `test:tools` — and therefore the LOCAL `npm run check` every session is told to run — reds for reasons unrelated to the change under test. ⚠ CI IS NOT AFFECTED, and that asymmetry is the point: `.github/workflows/tests.yml:164` runs the same selftests in a SEQUENTIAL bash `for` loop, one tool at a time, so it never creates the contention. The defect is invisible to CI by construction and lands entirely on the local gate | Reproduced TWICE on 2026-09-03 on unrelated branches: `tools/dsp-review-qwen.mjs` (it invokes a local model) TIMED OUT after 120 s inside the sweep at start-loads 3.95 and 7.93 — yet passes STANDALONE with `21 ok, 0 failed` at load 5.53, which is HIGHER than one of the failing runs. So machine load is NOT the discriminator and the flake is not environmental: the sweep manufactures its own contention. ⚠ The instrument that made this legible is `selftest-all`'s own `[load N at the kill, M at start]` stamp (#2093) — without it this reads as a broken tool. Candidate fixes, cheapest first: a concurrency cap (~cores) around the `Promise.all`; or a per-tool timeout scaled by observed fan-out; NOT simply raising 120 s, which hides the fan-out rather than fixing it. ⚠ A SECOND, INDEPENDENT trap met while diagnosing this, worth the line because it generalises past npm: verifying the other stages by hand I typed `verify:residue` when the script is `verify:residue-ids`, and `npm run <nonexistent>` exits NON-ZERO — so a MISSING target and a FAILING target are the same exit code. Identical in make and cargo. I nearly reported a phantom red on my own branch; check the script exists before believing its failure | fixed #2171 |

The two kinds are not mechanically separable: one says WHY IT WOULD FAIL, the other says WHAT WAS MEASURED. Tractable shape is (a) rephrase the 255 call sites as measurements and (b) gate new failure-phrased details. Found by Magpie chasing them AS vacuous gates, then retracting that reading after going to the assertion source | fixed #2178 |

| 2026-09-02-pat-applied-works-by-accident | 2026-09-02 | `PAT-RESIDUAL-ATTRIBUTION-2026-08-28-BRIEF.md` | a working path that works BY ACCIDENT — `pat-drift-attribution.mjs:105` reads `hostAxis.applied === true ? 0 : hostAxis.ppm`, PpgDex never writes `applied` (ECGDex does, at `applied: ecgAxisApplied` and `applied: false`), so the PPG leg always takes the raw-ppm arm on `undefined`. That arm is CORRECT, because `ppgdex-dsp.js` applies the ppm correction to `fs` and then rounds `fs` to 2 dp on the next line, erasing it at every magnitude this tool predicts. The defect is the accident, not the output: the next session to write `applied` truthfully zeroes a contribution that is really present and every test stays green. **OWNER DECISION** — two dispositions, fix the quantisation then write `applied` truthfully, or write `applied` to reflect what survived the rounding; 2 dp `fs` is a shipped-format question and `fs` feeds beat placement, so widening it is a measurement with a pre-stated bar, not a constant change | at 55 Hz one 0.01 Hz step is 181.8 ppm (half-step 90.9); corrections of 15/20/46 ppm move `fs` by 0.00082/0.00110/0.00253 Hz, all under the 0.005 half-step. `grep -c applied ppgdex-dsp.js` = 6, all prose, none a key. | 2026-09-04-respacc-empty-run-exits-zero | 2026-09-04 | `tools/resp-acc-headless.mjs` | A run in which no night scores writes NO figures, renders the accuracy table as a header with zero rows, and still exits 0. Measured on the full 79-night corpus: three `drew nothing (no figure written)` lines, an empty `--figures` directory, and `EXIT=0`. A caller cannot distinguish "the corpus produced no scoreable night" from "the run worked" by exit code or by the presence of output, so an unattended or CI invocation reads a total non-result as success. An honest zero — `0 of 79 nights scored` as an explicit verdict, and a non-zero exit — is the shape wanted; the refusal to score off-model nights is correct and is NOT what this row is about. | 79 nights staged (284 ACC + 205 CPAP), 564 table rows, 0 console errors, EXIT=0, `/tmp/mrr-figs-full` empty; the same tool on 3 and on 10 nights emits all three PNGs | fixed #2207 |

| 2026-09-05-sync-main-skips-while-root-dirty | 2026-09-05 | `CLAUDE.md` | CLAUDE.md:636 and `.claude/hooks/guard-stale-brief.sh` both misstate the guard’s failure direction. The hook's correctness rests on the shared root sitting AT `origin/main` — its own header says `base..origin/main` is then "empty BY CONSTRUCTION" because `tepna-sync-main.timer` fast-forwards it every 15 min. But that timer REFUSES while the root has uncommitted paths ("never sync over someone's work", which is correct and must not be changed), and a dirty root is the normal state here. So the precondition lapses silently: the hook keeps computing `merge-base(root HEAD, origin/main)` from a stale HEAD and reports commits a fresh worktree ALREADY contains. It over-reports, while the hook's own docs and CLAUDE.md:636 both say it "can only under-report" — that sentence is true of a stale `origin/main` and false of a stale HEAD. | 2026-09-05 02:15 on rig-x870: `journalctl --user -u tepna-sync-main` logs `SKIP — 7 uncommitted/untracked path(s)` for `~/Tepna` and `SKIP — 370` for `/srv/data/Tepna`, every run, while `systemctl show` reports `Result=success` and the timer shows as healthy — the skip is invisible above the log. Root `HEAD` measured **42 commits** behind `origin/main` at 02:15 with 7 dirty paths. ⚠️ THE LAG OSCILLATES — never quote it without a timestamp: Heron measured **103** at ~19:00 and both figures were correct when taken, because the timer DOES fast-forward whenever the root is momentarily clean, after which the root dirties again and drifts back. So the steady state is 'skips while dirty, and dirty is normal', not a monotonic lapse, and a reader sampling twice gets two answers with no error in either. Concrete instance of the mechanism: when the hook blocked Heron, the merge-base it used was `68ca74a7` — Heron's OWN commit from ~7 h earlier, i.e. the point where the root's HEAD had stopped moving. Heron read the symptom as a cwd/PreToolUse ordering problem; the ordering is real but incidental, and the two diagnoses imply different fixes. | fixed #2262 |
| 2026-09-05-drawn-migration-would-disable-h10 | 2026-09-05 | `#2212` | The proposed ECGDex migration in `2026-09-04-drawn-axis-consumers-unmigrated` would DISABLE the H10 host-axis correction on real data, and the row that proposed it says the opposite. Closed row `2026-09-04-drawn-rule-defeated-by-rounding` states that adding `&& !deviceDrawn` to `ecgdex-dsp.js:4648`/`:4678` is "SAFE on real H10 data (it would not fire)" — that holds only against a 99 % cut, and `deviceDrawn` is not gated at 99 % | `clock.js:454` CK_AXIS_DRAWN_SHARE is **0.67** (since #1252, 2026-08-14) and the same row measures the H10 device column at **98.63-98.65 %** top-delta share, so `deviceDrawn` is TRUE, not false. The populations are the same one: `ecgdex-dsp.js:4588-4591` builds `ecgAxisAnchors` as one anchor PER PARSED ROW from `devMs`, and `clock.js:546` tallies deltas over exactly those anchors — so the share the row measured on the raw rows is the share `deviceDrawn` sees. Consequence: the migration as written turns off `fs` correction for every real H10 recording, silently, which is the opposite of its stated intent. NOT a claim that the migration is wrong — a drawn axis genuinely should not be spent as a second clock — but its SAFETY argument is void and the ECGDex fixture re-cut the row calls "the real work" is not the whole of it. ⚠ Not re-measured here: no `Polar_H10_*_ECG.txt` is in this checkout (5 raw device files, all 1.81-39.28 %), so this rests on the closed row's own figure. Re-derive against a real H10 ECG fragment before migrating | fixed #2230 |

| 2026-09-05-playwright-blocked-by-apparmor-userns | 2026-09-05 | `tools/resp-acc-headless.mjs` | Every Playwright tool in the repo stopped being able to launch a browser on the primary dev box mid-session, and the failure Playwright surfaces names neither the cause nor the remedy — `browser.newPage: Target page, context or browser has been closed`, which reads like a crash or a resource problem. #2220 tells the next session this harness runs in 74 s; on this box it currently cannot start | Chromium states the cause itself when run directly: `FATAL:zygote_host_impl_linux.cc(128) No usable sandbox! If you are running on Ubuntu 23.10+ or another Linux distro that has disabled unprivileged user namespaces with AppArmor`. Confirmed by sysctl — `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns = 1` (while `kernel.unprivileged_userns_clone = 1`, so the plain userns sysctl is a red herring). Reproduced minimally: `chromium.launch()` RESOLVES and `newPage()` then fails, so a caller sees a dead browser rather than a refused one. Launching with `--no-sandbox --disable-gpu --disable-dev-shm-usage` works. Scope is wider than resp-acc: `tools/trio-power-headless.mjs` and `tools/frozen-timeline-check.mjs` use the same launch, and any local browser-gate run does too — CI is unaffected (its runners do not carry this AppArmor policy). ⚠ It WORKED TWICE EARLIER THE SAME DAY, so the policy changed under a running session; do not infer from an old green that it works now. ⚠ NOT a Tepna defect and NOT resource exhaustion — I first guessed memory pressure and that was wrong; the binary renders a DOM fine under `--headless --dump-dom`. Remedy is an AppArmor profile for the playwright chromium binary (system config, outside this repo) or an opt-in `--no-sandbox` path in the tools; a retry-once fallback was written during #2225 and reverted as unproven, since it fixed the launch and one 1.3 GB run still died mid-ingest for reasons not established | fixed #2749 |
| 2026-09-05-drawn-share-is-anchor-level | 2026-09-05 | `#2216` | My own row `2026-09-05-drawn-migration-would-disable-h10` is WRONG and would have blocked a correct migration. It argued that `deviceDrawn` fires on real H10 ECG because the device column concentrates at 98.63-98.65 percent, well above the 0.67 cut. Measured through the real code path: it does not fire, anywhere. The original claim in `2026-09-04-drawn-rule-defeated-by-rounding` — that adding `&& !deviceDrawn` to `ecgdex-dsp.js` is SAFE on real H10 data — stands | Measured 2026-09-05 by spying on `DexClock.hostAxis` return values while `ECGDSP.parseECG` ran on real files (the real function on the real anchors, not a reimplementation): 25 `Polar_H10_*_ECG.txt` across 25 DISTINCT dates, `drawnShare` 0.0038 to 0.0677, `deviceDrawn` false 25 of 25 — an order of magnitude below 0.67. THE ERROR, because it is reusable: 98.6 percent is the concentration of raw per-SAMPLE deltas, while `hostAxis` computes `drawnShare` over its ANCHORS, and anchors are only the rows carrying a host stamp — a sparse subset (n = 41 to 6924 anchors on files of hundreds of thousands of samples), whose deltas are irregular. Two different populations, one name. I did check the code and read `ecgdex-dsp.js:4588-4591` as building one anchor per parsed ROW; `parsedAnchors` is built from `anchors`, which are stamp-bearing rows only, so I stopped one level above the fact and that level looked like confirmation. Note also that ECGDex does not carry `drawnShare`/`deviceDrawn` on its own `hostAxis` summary at all — independent confirmation of `2026-09-04-drawn-axis-consumers-unmigrated` | fixed #2237 |
| 2026-09-05-mutate-diff-timeout-reads-as-kill | 2026-09-05 | `capture-host/tools/mutate_diff.py` | The diff-scoped Python mutation gate treats a mutant that TIMED OUT (or came back `suspicious`) as killed: `mutate_diff.py:312` keeps only `: survived` lines of `mutmut results`, on the premise (comment at :291) that the listing holds "survivors and not-checked ONLY" — but mutmut's `results()` prints every non-killed status (`timeout`, `suspicious`, `no tests`, `skipped`; `mutmut/__main__.py:1556-1565`). Two false verdicts follow. (a) An unclassified mutant on a changed line that times out under load is absent from `survivors`, so the gate reports "every mutant on the changed functions was killed" about a mutant no test saw. (b) `classify` derives REFUTED as generated-but-not-survived, so a correct equivalence entry whose mutant timed out is reported as "the mutant was KILLED, so a distinguishing input exists" — an instruction to delete a right entry. | Same tree, two runs: CI run 33967414946 job 101309892792 (#2219) excused all 11 `nightqc.py` entries and reported exactly 2 required survivors (`x_rtc_drift_summary__mutmut_3`/`_6`); a local run on the loaded dev box 2026-09-05 printed `⏰ 28` and `10 equivalence entr(y/ies) REFUTED` for the same entries, including `- span_h = None` whose assignment is dead on every path. The sibling full-audit driver already states the invariant this breaks — the per-mutant-timeout changeset in `changes/` (dated 2026-08-30): "a mutant that DOES exceed it is recorded `timeout` — that is unmeasured, never killed, so a tighter bound cannot manufacture a pass" — and `mutate_diff.py` is the driver that manufactures exactly that pass. Fix shape: parse the status word, count `timeout`/`suspicious` as UNDECIDED (required, never killed, never refuting), and re-run those mutants once before reporting. | fixed #2403 |

Both choices are DELIBERATE and documented — the shell corpus because a helper that calls a function does wire it, the bare name because a callback reference has no parens — so the remedy is a hypothesis rather than an obvious fix: candidates are excluding comment/string context in the shell corpus, or naming the MATCHING FILE beside a stale entry so the collision costs one grep instead of an hour of bisecting | reproduced on `origin/main` 21312e30: `adapter_ab.compare` is among 29 `orphan_functions`; appending one line containing the bare word `compare` to `tepna-update.sh` drops it to 28 and `find_unwired --check` exits 1 with `ALLOW_FUNCS['compare'] — adapter_ab analysis tool`, naming a function and a module absent from the diff. Reverting the line restores green. Cost ~20 min and four wrong theories — base drift, `.sh` is not scanned, `importers()`, and ⚠️ **"it was already broken on main"**, which is the one to warn the next reader off: it is the only theory that ENDS the investigation, it explains every symptom, and it is what a reasonable person concludes when a gate names a file absent from their diff. What refutes it costs one command — run the scan on a PRISTINE checkout of the same base and diff the two orphan sets; here that isolated the cause to a single element. Do not accept "pre-existing" about a red you have not reproduced without your change | fixed #2763 |
| 2026-09-05-monitor-chip-registries | 2026-09-05 | `capture-host/tests/test_monitor_chip_scope.py` | Two lists of monitor chip-function NAMES are hand-kept in node-only test files — `test_monitor_chip_scope.CHIPS` and the stub prelude in `test_monitor_device_cards._render` — so a chip added to `renderRemembered`'s template and absent from either list fails ONLY where node is installed. On the capture box those files skip, and a skip is reported beside a green summary, so the author sees no signal at all; the miss surfaces on someone else's machine or in CI. This is not the missing-comment defect it first looks like: a comment mitigates it for whoever reads that line, while the lists remain two places that must agree with a third (the template) by hand. Remedy is a HYPOTHESIS, not a spec — derive CHIPS from the template itself (`${(\w+Chip)(d)}` over `monitor.html`) and assert every derived name is also stubbed in `_render`'s prelude, so a new chip cannot be absent from either list and reds in CI whether or not its author had node. Whoever picks this up re-derives it | Measured 2026-09-05 on `claude/oxy-storm-status-r5t` (PR #2256): adding `oxyStormChip` to `monitor.html` left both lists untouched; on vigil `pytest tests/test_monitor_storm_chip.py` reported `4 skipped` and `check.sh` summarised pytest ok with `TOTAL 100%` branch coverage, while the same tree on a node-having rig failed 5 tests in `test_monitor_device_cards.py` with `ReferenceError: oxyStormChip is not defined`. `grep -n 'CHIPS = \[' capture-host/tests/test_monitor_chip_scope.py` -> `:87` | fixed #2753 |
| 2026-09-06-inherited-global-adapter-not-map-resolved | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/capture.py` | the INHERITED global `adapter:` is taken RAW while a device's OWN `adapter:` is resolved through the `adapters:` map, so the two forms are not interchangeable in this one position. `instance_devices` reads `mac = resolve_adapter_name(cfg, spec) if spec else (cfg or {}).get("adapter")` — the `else` arm never reaches the map. A global written as a DECLARED NAME therefore inherits nothing, and every device that relies on inheritance becomes unowned. An operator can reasonably write it: `resolve_adapter_name`'s own docstring says names exist *"so the config and the systemd unit read the same way"*, and a raw global is the single place that promise is not kept. **Remedy is a HYPOTHESIS** — the direction to take is map-resolving the inherited global exactly as a device's own spec is resolved, on the strength of that docstring; it is a behaviour change rather than a test, which is why it is a row and not a fix. It is a ROW rather than an incident because it fails LOUDLY: `unowned_devices` names the device and the caller logs it at startup, so a capture never quietly serves fewer devices than its config names. The pin test added in #2267 is what a fix would flip | measured 2026-09-06 on two configs differing ONLY in the form of the global, same adapters map and same single adapter-less device: `adapter: 00:01:95:CC:53:02` gives `instance_devices(cfg,'sena')` = ['d1'] and `unowned_devices` = []; `adapter: sena` gives `instance_devices` = [] and `unowned_devices` = ['d1']. Pinned by `test_an_inherited_global_named_rather_than_a_mac_does_not_inherit` in `capture-host/tests/test_adapter_pin.py` | fixed #2270 |
| 2026-09-05-pgrep-selfmatch-kills-too | 2026-09-05 | `CLAUDE.md` | §👥.4 warns that `pgrep -f <pattern>` matches the shell running it, but states the hazard only for WAITING (`until ! pgrep -f …` blocking on itself) and prescribes remedies for waiting alone — own the PID, wait on a sentinel. The same self-match makes any LIST-THEN-ACT pipeline kill its own shell, which is immediate rather than eventual and destroys the acting process instead of hanging it, so none of §4's remedies apply and its bracket-trick caveat does not either. Measured three times in one session, in three forms that each read as different bugs: `pkill -f storm-watch.sh`; `for p in $(pgrep -f nrf_sniffer_ble); do kill $p; done`; and a `case` pattern containing the literal `timeout -s INT`. The third is the instructive one — the pattern was a SHELL CASE LABEL, not an argument to any process tool, so the text need only APPEAR in the command line. Remedy is a hypothesis: extend §4 to name the kill case and prescribe the two-command discipline — list matching PIDs in one command, read them, then kill numeric PIDs in a second command whose text contains no pattern at all | Three exit-144 (128+SIGTERM) terminations of my own Bash tool call on vigil 2026-09-05 between 20:30 and 20:38 UTC-4, each ending the shell before its kill list was applied; the second left the O2Ring air-witness capture stopped and the third left four `storm-watch.sh` instances racing one `/dev/ttyACM0`, requiring a list-read-kill recovery. §👥.4's own text: "the waiter itself runs as `bash -c '… until ! pgrep -f …'`, so its own `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` CONTAINS the pattern it is searching for" — the same premise, a different consequence | OPEN |

It is a LANE-ASYMMETRY trap rather than a bug in any one file: each shape is fine alone. **Remedy is a hypothesis** — normalise the three onto one shape (bare global plus `module.exports`, which two of the three already do), or give the set a single loader; either is a change to files other tests import, so it is a work-unit and not a footnote. | 2026-09-05-content-gate-diffs-from-a-stale-marker | 2026-09-05 | `capture-host/tepna-update.sh` | The C4 content gate decides a restart is owed by diffing `<marker>..HEAD -- capture-host/`, but the marker records what the UPDATER last deployed, not what is RUNNING — a restart by anyone else leaves it behind. Measured on vigil 2026-09-05: marker `31064194` (mtime 19:58) while the running process served `487faf9c` after three hand restarts at 20:21:36-20:22:02. The gate then sees capture-host content between marker and HEAD that the running code ALREADY has, judges a restart owed, and — because the recording interlock only DEFERS it — fires at the first moment recording goes false, which mid-night is a brief doff rather than session end. The updater's own header bounds this as at most one redundant restart into identical code and that bound predates C4; what C4 adds is that the redundant restart is now also CONTENT-JUSTIFIED, so nothing downstream can tell it from a real one. `/api/version` already reports the running sha, which is the quantity the diff actually wants | marker `31064194` + mtime 19:58 vs `/api/version` `487faf9c` and `ActiveEnterTimestamp 20:22:02`, both read on the box 2026-09-05; `journalctl -u tepna-update --since 20:20 --until 20:25` empty, so the restarts were not the updater's | fixed #2751 |
| 2026-09-05-capture-status-joins-two-key-namespaces | 2026-09-05 | `capture-host/capture_status.py` | `render()` joins a device's CONFIGURED stream names against the telemetry bus's stream KEYS — two different namespaces — and its fallback `stream_by_key.get(k, {"key": k})` turns an unmatched name into `active=None`, which is indistinguishable from a stream that is genuinely idle. Measured against the live box: bus keys are `acc_vs ppg_vs ppi_vs acc_h10 hr_h10 o2ppg o2ppg2w acc_o2 …` while the configured names are `acc ppg ppi` (Verity), `ecg acc hr` (H10) and `spo2 ppg ppg2w acc` (ring). The Verity matches NOTHING, so it can never read STREAMING and shows `connected (idle)` while writing 16.8 MB with a 2 s-old mtime; the H10 and ring read STREAMING only by accident, each having exactly one stream whose config name equals its bus key (`ecg`, `spo2`), and their other five streams are permanently `active=None`. The tool's whole docstring is that connected alone is not capture, so a false idle on a device writing megabytes inverts its headline claim. ⚠️ The two accidental matches are NOT stable: every other bus key carries a device suffix, so the obvious tidy-up (`spo2_o2`, `ecg_h10`) would leave ALL FOUR devices reporting idle forever and the tool announcing a box that captures nothing while it captures everything — the collision is currently the only thing keeping its headline claim partly true. Fix: match the namespaces, and render an unmatched name AS unmatched rather than as not-active | live `/api/state` 2026-09-05: `streams[].key` vs `devices[].streams` — Verity 0 of 3 matched, H10 1 of 3, ring 1 of 4; `capture_status.py:50` | fixed #2743 |

The repo's dominant class: a check that ran and examined nothing | Reproduced in isolation by planting each event before a single test — both yield `sleeps=400 waits_seen=0`, byte-identical to the full-suite failure of `test_run_viatom_publishes_a_DOWN_link_while_it_waits_out_a_stall` (#2273's plant, which caught it only because it asserts a non-empty observation). 8 direct `.set()` sites across `tests/test_capture_runners.py`, `test_capture_coverage_100.py`, `test_cpap_spool_wire.py`, `test_cpap_poller.py` | fixed #2279 |

The failure is asymmetric and therefore expensive: a reader who follows the reference and finds nothing concludes the CLAIM is false — that the artifact was never built or was removed — when only the coordinate rotted. Same class as `2026-09-06-docs-index-role-disagrees-with-brief-header`: a fact recorded on one surface that nothing keeps true. Compounding it, the obvious recovery grep also fails: searching `withdraw` misses both sites because the code says `WITHDREW`, so a reader who checks by grep gets a second false negative and is now twice as confident the note is gone. Both notes verified present. Scope of the pattern is unmeasured — no one has counted `file:line` citations across `briefs/`, and that count is the first thing the fix needs | → `BRIEF-CODE-ANCHOR-CHECK-2026-09-11-BRIEF.md` |

A writer opened, written to and never closed does not raise, does not warn and does not lose the file: it loses the UNFLUSHED TAIL of every session with the header and nearly all rows present, the same "night reads as complete while its tail is missing" loss S1 calls the charter's unforgivable failure, reached by a missed registry entry instead of a swallowed errno. | 2026-09-06-cpap-pin-names-unbonded-adapter | 2026-09-06 | `VIGIL-BLUETOOTH-ADAPTERS-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md` | `cpap.ble_stream.adapter` (line 135 of the box's gitignored `config.yaml`) pins the CPAP to the Intel AX210 `28:0C:50:0C:18:FD`, on which the AS11 is NOT bonded — `Paired: no / Bonded: no`, advertising as the generic `ResMed Device`, while the Sena `00:01:95:CC:53:02` (the global default) holds `Paired: yes / Bonded: yes` as `ResMed 590541`. The AS11's proprietary service needs an encrypted link, so `BleakCharacteristicNotFoundError` on `a6220003-35f1-4b20-afae-cb089d2044aa` is the EXPECTED result of an unbonded connection rather than a fault, and every CPAP poll fails over onto the reserved wearables radio — the load the pin exists to keep off it. OWNER DECISION 2026-09-06: pair the AS11 on the Intel and KEEP the pin there; re-pointing the pin at the Sena is foreclosed because the Intel is the permanent fixture while the other dongles may become Zephyr/nRF sniffer parts for timing, which probably cannot carry the encrypted link. Pairing is owner-attended. ⚠️ TWO ATTRIBUTIONS WERE WITHDRAWN and are recorded so they are not redrawn from the same numbers: that this explains #2170's 758 failovers (it cannot — through Sep 04 the pin was the TP-Link and the Intel was the adapter that ANSWERED, 700+ times; the pin flipped Sep 05, so these are two regimes), and the prediction that a remedy would drop failovers to ~zero (unsupported — the current pin already sits at 60 then 15 against the old pin's 136-168, so a null result would be misread as exonerating the bond) | failovers by day x adapter pair: Aug31-Sep04 `AC:A7:F1:29:9D:1D -> 28:0C:50:0C:18:FD` 166/148/161/168/136, Sep05-06 `28:0C:50:0C:18:FD -> 00:01:95:CC:53:02` 60/15. AS11 poll failures by day x exception: Char 157/127/8/13/10/9/6/9/3/1 and DeviceNotFound 80/358/547/2/39/3/2/32/0/29 for Aug28..Sep06. ⚠️ Aug29 127 Char -> Aug30 8 Char with DeviceNotFound spiking to 547 is a REAL discontinuity and is deliberately left UNEXPLAINED; Aug30 onward is one continuous single-digit Char regime, not a recovery from zero (a first pass reported 0 there, which was a `tail -16` truncation of a 23-row table, not a measurement). Bond states and both tables read directly off the box 2026-09-06 | fixed #2376 |
| 2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes | 2026-09-06 | `CAPTURE-FILESET-RESUME-2026-08-19-BRIEF.md` | The resume decision was built for `run_polar` alone and the O2Ring was never wired to it, so the ring still mints a fresh file-set across a five-second reconnect. `resumable_stamp` occurs ONCE in the tree (`capture.py`, inside `run_polar`); `run_oxyii` takes `started = _now()` and derives all six of its filenames from it. The brief SCOPES the ring rather than excluding it — §1's denominator is 76 device-nights across three devices and the 28.3x headline includes the ring's 376 sets, §2 cites the ring's own PPG grid writer as the MODEL for honest gaps, and §4's out-of-scope names only retroactive merging and `drop_not_worn`. The "the ring never resumes ... `O2PpgGrid` is rebuilt per session by design, precisely because a ring reconnect opens a new file" sentence was written at box-1 CLOSURE (#2138), not at scoping, and is circular: it justifies the file behaviour by the grid behaviour and the grid behaviour by the file behaviour. Verdict from the coordinator, 2026-09-06: it fell out. The DONE is earned for the Polar path and silent on a third of its own denominator — do NOT reopen it. ⚠️ THE ONE-LINE FIX IS A TRAP, twice over: three ring writers open `"w"` (`Spo2CsvWriter`, `OxyFrameLogWriter`, `RingClockLogWriter`) where `StreamWriter` and `PmdArrivalLogWriter` already do `"a" if resumed`, so adding the call alone TRUNCATES the file it resumes onto (#2166 one writer over); and `O2PpgGrid` is constructed per session inside `run_oxyii`, and since the ring has no device clock that grid IS the `sensor timestamp [ns]` column, so resuming without carrying or re-seeding it puts two overlapping synthesized timelines in one file — a Clock-Contract fabrication, strictly worse than the fragmentation. Fix is three parts: the resume decision at the same single chokepoint `run_polar` uses, resume-awareness in those three writers, and the grid carried across the reconnect advancing over the reconnect gap — measured on the host stamp, or bridged with the ring's own `duration_s` counter (u32 at [0:4] of every 0x04 frame, logged beside the host stamp in `OXYFRAME.txt`), whose rate is bimodal and stall-prone (measured by Wren 2026-09-06, 653 segments: ≥1 h segments split −0…−16 ppm vs −2270…−4591 ppm, the slow mode tracking the counter-stall fraction; row to follow in Wren's duration-counter PR). The grid already implements exactly that for in-session REAL LOSS, so this extends a mechanism rather than inventing one | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06 over the 15 post-#1532 nights (2026-08-23 →), signal-carrying sets only. Verity median **2** sets/night and H10 **2** — already at minimum — against O2Ring median **13**, total 531, max 320. Of the ring's 524 inter-chunk gaps, **94.1 % are under the 300 s resume window, median 5 s** (p25 4 s, p75 5 s): nearly every boundary is one the window exists to collapse. Re-counting each night as 1 + gaps ≥ 300 s projects **531 → 47 sets, 11.3x**, median/night 35.4 → 3.1, landing the ring where the Polars already sit; the 2026-08-29 storm night goes 320 → 4. ⚠️ SPLIT AT THE STORM-HOLD DEPLOY, because the projection's basis moves: #2209 merged 2026-09-05 09:05 UTC, and pre-hold is 499 sets / median gap 5 s / 94.6 % inside the window while post-hold is 32 sets / median gap 38 s / **76.0 % (n = 25 gaps)**. The hold correctly fragments — a 900 s hold exceeds the 300 s window by design — so quote 76 % for post-deploy behaviour, not 94 %, and re-measure over more post-hold nights before sizing the work. Four rival causes were measured and rejected: daemon restarts account for only 12.3 % of chunk starts (within 90 s of a `Tepna_*` start); adapter contention is refuted because the Verity and H10 share that radio on the same nights at median 2; the `drop_not_worn` duty cycle WAS the dominant cause but is confined to 2026-08-10 → 08-18 (788 of 793 Verity runs in the 90–105 s bin) and was fixed by #1532; link instability is real but produces 5-second gaps, not new file-sets. ⚠️ 82 % of the all-time totals predate #1532, so anyone reading the lifetime column will re-derive the closed `drop_not_worn` defect and propose a fix for it | fixed #2378 |
| 2026-09-06-ring-connect-attempts-mostly-fail | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/capture.py` | Roughly three of every four O2Ring connect attempts never reach a session, and nothing surfaces it as a rate. The lifecycle log records the transitions faithfully, so the fact is available, but no gate, digest or status field reduces them to "what fraction of attempts became a session" — and a failure rate is invisible in a per-event log by construction: each line looks like a retry that eventually worked. Compounding it, the OXYLIFE `failure` column is essentially unused (ONE `transport_failure` across fourteen nights), so the attempts that fail do not record WHY they failed, and the classification that would separate a device asleep from a radio problem from a contended adapter does not exist even in principle from these files. This is a data-YIELD defect rather than a fragmentation one and is probably the larger term of the two: a set that never opens loses the whole interval, where a fragmented set loses only its boundary. Distinct from `2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes`, which is about how sessions are FILED once they succeed | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06 from `OXYLIFE.csv` across the 14 nights that carry it (2026-08-24 → 09-06): **1216 `disconnected → connecting [scan + connect]` against 383 `connecting → connected [auth + setup]`** — 31.5 % of attempts reach a session, so **888 (73 %) end as `connecting → disconnected [session ended]` without ever connecting**. The `failure` column is populated on exactly **1** row in the same corpus. Not measured: whether the failure rate differs by hour, by adapter, by ring battery, or between storm and non-storm periods — each of those is a plausible discriminator and none has been cut | fixed #2377 |
| 2026-09-06-capture-daemon-restarts-nightly | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/deploy/tepna-capture.service` | The capture daemon starts many times a night on a box that is never rebooted, and no artifact reports the count. Each start is visible only as another `Tepna_*_CLOCK.csv`/`_LINK.csv` pair in the night directory, which reads as ordinary bookkeeping rather than as a restart; the number is recoverable but nobody recovers it. A restart is not harmless even when the supervisor brings the process straight back: it ends every device's in-flight session at once, so it is an upper bound on how long ANY link can be held, and a device that could otherwise stream all night cannot. Whether these are crashes, watchdog cycles, deploys from `tepna-update.timer`, or intentional supervision is UNDETERMINED — this row records the rate and the absence of the reason, not a diagnosis | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06 by counting distinct `Tepna_<14-digit>_` session stamps per night directory over the 44 nights from 2026-07-25: **min 3, median 12.5, max 36 starts per night**. Bounding what it explains, so it is not over-read: only **12.3 %** of device chunk starts fall within 90 s of a daemon start (O2Ring 23 %, Verity 5.7 %, H10 13.1 %), so restarts are a contributing term and not the cause of the fragmentation in `2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes`. Not measured: the exit codes, the `Restart=` policy the unit actually carries, or whether the rate correlates with deploys — `journalctl -u tepna-capture` holds the answer and rotates, so the first step of any pickup is to capture it before it ages out | fixed #2751 |
| 2026-09-06-ring-duration-counter-bimodal | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/capture.py` | The O2Ring's `duration_s` counter — u32 LE at [0:4] of every `0x04` frame, already written to `OXYFRAME.txt` beside the host stamp — is a real device oscillator, and the repo recorded it as having no frequency at all. `O2PpgGrid`'s premise sentence said "The ring has NO device clock" and that phrasing propagated into three briefs and a test docstring as a fact about the HARDWARE (corrected in the same PR as this row); six other sites say the same words scoped to the sample stream or the files and are CORRECT — the `sensor timestamp [ns]` column really is drawn, and `O2RING-SYNTHESISED-AXIS`'s whole thesis stands. ⚠️ THE CORRECTION IS NOT "THE RING HAS A USABLE CLOCK", and the caution that produced the wrong sentence was substantively right: the counter's rate is BIMODAL. Half of long nights track the host to under 16 ppm — ordinary crystal behaviour — and the other half run −2270…−4591 ppm, which is not a slower oscillator but a counter that fails to advance on some host seconds. Nothing should consume it as a timing source until that mode is detectable at runtime; a consumer that averages the two gets a confident number describing neither. It cannot place samples in any case (1 Hz against 125 Hz), so the PPG grid is unaffected and its design is unchanged — the value is as a RATE reference over hours, and as the only thing that can bridge a reconnect the host cannot measure from inside the gap (see `2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes`) | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06 from `OXYFRAME.txt` across 2026-08/09, 653 counter segments split at every reset. **The rate is span-dependent, which explains the prior numbers rather than contradicting them**: median ppm by span is **+15590 (<1 min, n=487) · −2739 (1–10 min, n=73) · −94.9 (10–60 min, n=38) · −58.3 (>1 h, n=55)** — a 1 s-quantised counter has no leverage on a short session, so `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH`'s 44-session median of +540 ppm is falsified as a DEVICE number by its own lack of a span condition, not by being mismeasured. **Among ≥1 h segments the distribution is bimodal**: one cluster at −0.0…−15.5 ppm, another at −2270…−4591, and `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH`'s −3446 "one atypical night" is the second cluster — about half of long nights, not an outlier; `PAT-PACKET-ARRIVAL` §6's 3851 ppm is the same mode seen once. **The discriminator is measurable**: counting host seconds in which the counter did not advance gives 0.00–0.05 % for the fast cluster against 0.19–0.36 % for the slow one, corresponding night by night, and one 8.06 h segment ends **64.7 s** behind the host. Availability: the counter is live for 76.7 % of covered wall time and **99 % of that is in segments ≥ 10 min** (12 segments, 7 over an hour, longest 9.83 h). It bridges a reconnect exactly when the ring's session survives it — measured on 2026-09-04, host gap 36.3 s against a counter advance of +36. ⚠️ **The MECHANISM of the stall is UNKNOWN and the first thing a pickup owes.** An attempt to characterise the drop structure was confounded by the quantisation sawtooth (251 ±1 s edges, 155 down / 96 up, netting +64.7 s — the edges are phase crossings, not the drops), so whether it is dropped ticks, a pausing counter, or firmware load is not established. Not measured: whether the mode correlates with recording state, battery, temperature, or poll rate; and every number here is the one unit `S8AW2100` | fixed #2297 |
| 2026-09-06-probe-mutation-survivors-are-fixture-limited | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/tests/test_probe_oxyii_0x03.py` | `probe_oxyii_0x03.py` carries 446 surviving mutants after #2294's hardening, and almost none of them should become `mutate-equivalence.json` entries — recording the count here instead, so the next session to touch `run()` knows what it is inheriting rather than rediscovering it. ⚠️ **FILING THESE AS EQUIVALENT WOULD BE A FALSE CLAIM.** That file's vocabulary is `no-distinguishing-input`, i.e. no test COULD tell the mutant from the original. These are a different thing: `asyncio.sleep(0.4)` → `sleep(1.4)`, `BleakClient(timeout=30)` → `timeout=31`, `response=False` → `True` are all distinguishable in principle and merely unobserved, because `_install` monkeypatches `asyncio.sleep` to a no-op and `_FakeRing` ignores the connection arguments. An equivalence entry for them would be a suppression that silences nothing while looking like it does — the exact failure the file's own stale-entry rule exists to prevent, committed deliberately. The honest remedies are a fixture that OBSERVES the argument (as #2294 did for the characteristic) or leaving the gap recorded and visible. Not urgent: `mutate_diff.py` is diff-scoped and advisory, so these gate nothing until someone edits the probe's source, at which point that PR's own diff pulls them in | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06, `tools/mutate_diff.py --base c9f4dc05~1` with mutmut 3.7.0: **446 survivors** — x_main 204 · x_run 171 · x_summarise 52 · x_marker_stats 10 · x_verdict 5 · x_parse_counts 4. Reconciled against the #2290 CI listing (140) rather than assumed comparable: the key space is IDENTICAL (highest `x_run` index 207 in both), so the difference is what each run reported, not what each generated. Of CI's 66 `x_run` survivors, **11 are killed in this run** — 47, 50, 53, 60, 64, 73, 90, 133, 200, 201, 204 — and that is 4 more than #2294 designed for: recording WHICH characteristic each write went to also observes the auth/setup write arguments, so mutants written off as fixture limits died as a side effect. Two indices (71, 72) appear here and not in the CI listing; both are `asyncio.sleep(0.4)`, and that call occurs TWICE (after auth, after setup), so the CI list captured one pair — a listing gap, not a regression. Also found while running it: **mutmut was absent from `requirements-dev.txt`** (CI installs it inline), so the gate refused to run from the documented local setup — fixed in the same PR. Not measured: whether the 204 `x_main` argparse-default survivors are worth any fixture at all, and whether `x_summarise`'s remaining 52 contain a second `>= cap`-class defect like the one the #2294 relay caught | OPEN |
| 2026-09-06-ring-duration-counts-data-not-time | 2026-09-06 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **Corrects `2026-09-06-ring-duration-counter-bimodal`, written earlier the same day by the same session.** That row said the ring's `duration_s` rate is bimodal — clean nights under 16 ppm, about half of long nights at −2270…−4591 ppm — and left the mechanism open. The bimodality is real as a description of a fitted line and wrong as a description of the counter: **`duration_s` counts SECONDS OF SIGNAL, not seconds of time.** The ring emits ~125.9 samples per DEVICE-second in both populations; what differs is how much signal it produced per WALL second. A single regression across a night whose loss is bursty renders that deficit as a rate, which is where this row's predecessor, `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH`'s −3446 ppm and `PAT-PACKET-ARRIVAL`'s 3851 ppm all come from — three independent measurements of the same artifact. The frames that fail to advance the counter carry ~73 PPG samples against ~127 normally and arrive in RUNS (62 of 71 adjacent), so they are short frames, not missed ticks. Consequences: (1) it can never be a timebase, because it does not measure time; (2) specifically it CANNOT bridge a reconnect gap for `2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes` — across a gap where the ring produced nothing it advances by nothing, so that row's fix must use the host stamp after all and the counter is not the shortcut it looked like; (3) it IS an exact data-completeness measure that nothing in the tree computes — `host_elapsed - device_advance` is seconds of missing signal, per night, for free from a column already on disk. Whether to publish that metric is an owner decision, not taken here | Measured on vigil 2026-09-06 from `OXYFRAME.txt`, ≥1 h monotonic segments. **Samples per device-second, slow vs fast populations: 125.87 · 125.92 · 124.93 · 125.71 against 125.80 · 124.53 · 125.85 · 125.62 · 125.45 — indistinguishable.** Device-advance vs host-elapsed: 35367/35370 s (clean, 9.82 h) against 31353/31422 s (slow, 8.73 h) and 25626/25709 s (slow, 7.14 h). Hour-by-hour on the −3687 ppm segment the whole-segment fit is shown to be an artifact: hours 0-3 and 8-9 run −30…+31 ppm — the SAME as a clean night — with the divergence confined to hour 4 (+50.0 s) and hours 5-7 (+11.9/+12.9/+13.8 s/h, bracketing the ~12.5 s/h already recorded in CLAUDE.md §7). The hour-4 rise contains **zero jumps > 1.5 s across 3585 frames**, so it is accumulation, not a step. Poll cadence is ruled out as the cause: mean host frame interval is 1.0041-1.0044 s in EVERY hour including the diverging ones, and the arithmetic closes exactly — predicted rise 50.1 s vs 50.0 measured (hour 4), 12.1 vs 11.9 (hour 5). Discriminator on the frames themselves: `ppg_n` is 73.29 ± 17.59 on non-advancing frames against 126.97 ± 3.51 on advancing ones, **15.3 sd**. ⚠️ NOT explained and deliberately not smoothed: one 2.22 h segment gives 118.27 samples/device-second against ~125.9 everywhere else, 6 % low, which this account does not cover. Also unexplained: what drives the loss bursts themselves — contact, motion, PI, battery and run_status show no separation at all, so the cause is not in the frame. Every number is the single unit `S8AW2100` | OPEN |

The item is "slow physiology (BP/vasomotor/posture/stage) vs an instrumental effect invisible to the host axis"; the oracle reports a per-night mode that is stable within a night and differs between nights, and **both candidates predict exactly that**. The half-width sweep already run (w = 50/200/300, #2029) is the strongest version of "run it again" and moved nothing, because widening a search does not add a discriminating input. Separating them needs a covariate that moves with physiology and not with the instrument — stage from the CPAP/EEG leg, posture from ACC, or a night where the instrument changes and the physiology does not — i.e. a designed experiment, not an execution. **The class:** an item can be perfectly well specified, name the right instrument, and still be unanswerable BY that instrument; "which instrument" and "can that instrument discriminate" are different questions and the brief only asked the first | Verified 2026-09-06 by re-reading §4's own framing ("§11's perfect-clock oracle is the instrument that answers this, not argument") against the corpus results recorded in the same brief: 43 nights, 4 SIGNAL RECOVERED / 20 PARTIAL / 5 NO RECOVERY / 0 UNDEFINED, modes 215/315/355/405 ms invariant across a 6× width sweep. Both briefs parked with the blocker and its owner named; the ACC and CPAP legs are local, so this is design work and not access | OPEN |

| 2026-09-06-link-substring-is-not-a-row-match | 2026-09-06 | `#2308` | WITHDRAWAL of row `2026-09-06-docs-index-role-disagrees-with-brief-header`, which is a FALSE POSITIVE — there is no header/Role disagreement, and the error was the query rather than the data. That row would have sized work that does not exist. The generalisable defect it accidentally demonstrates is worth keeping: a sweep that locates a table row by SUBSTRING (`grep -m1 "briefs/<name>)"`) matches the FIRST line containing that link, and a brief's link also appears inside OTHER briefs' description cells — so it compared the PARENT `PPG-FOOT-PLACEMENT-2026-09-01` row's Role cell (DONE) against the CHILD `-FOLLOWUPS-` brief's header (IN-PROGRESS) and reported a disagreement between two different documents. A row must be anchored to the START of the line (`^\| \[\`<name>\`\]\(`), never found by substring | Re-measured row-anchored across all **481** briefs carrying a DOCS-INDEX row: **0 mismatches**, so the population is clean and always was. `git log --since=2026-09-06 -- DOCS-INDEX.md` shows the cited row did not change that day, which is what separates "my sweep was wrong" from "it was real and someone fixed it". ⚠️ A SECOND ERROR ON TOP: the first withdrawal attempt EDITED the wrong row's evidence cell, which `verify:residue-ids` refuses (only the state cell may change) — and it passed LOCALLY because the base `origin/main` predated the row's own merge, so a mutation read as an add. Re-running after a fetch reproduced CI's failure. The tool's own message carries the correct procedure and this row follows it: a wrong row gets a NEW row saying so | fixed #2865 |
| 2026-09-06-warmup-verdict-goes-stale | 2026-09-06 | `PPG-ABSENCE-AS-VALUE-2026-09-06-BRIEF.md` | The zero-order-hold class is decided per channel over the FIRST `HELD_WARMUP_RUNS` = 64 runs and then never revisited, so a channel whose character changes mid-night keeps a verdict taken from its opening minutes. A stream that starts as a hold and later varies stays `held` and has its sub-`T_STUCK` rows suppressed for the rest of the recording; the reverse leaves a `variable` verdict on a stream that became a hold, which merely over-reports. The mitigation shipped is VISIBILITY, not correction: a `# final channel=… total_runs=… mean_run=… class=…` line carries the whole-night distribution beside the warm-up verdict, so a disagreement is readable — but nothing acts on it, and no consumer is required to check. | Read at `writers.py` `_RunSidecar._decide` (called once per channel, guarded by `channel not in self.klass`) on 2026-09-06. The remedy is a hypothesis and should be measured before it is built — candidates are re-deciding on a rolling window (costs the property that a verdict is stable for a file) or emitting a second verdict line at close and letting the consumer reconcile (costs nothing live, moves the work to WU-6). Runs ≥ `T_STUCK` are unconditional, so the worst case cannot hide the failure mode itself | fixed #2842 |
| 2026-09-06-t-stuck-validated-on-ring-only | 2026-09-06 | `PPG-ABSENCE-AS-VALUE-2026-09-06-BRIEF.md` | `T_STUCK = 200` is validated on **O2Ring 8-bit pleth only** — 3.16 M samples across two `_PPG.txt` files, non-sentinel runs ≥ 200 = 1 per file, legitimate ≥ 200 = zero. The same constant is applied by `RUN_MIN_BY_STREAM` to four other streams whose plateau distributions have never been measured: the Verity's 3-LED `ppg` (different ADC, different bit depth, wrist site), `ppg2w` (the ring's raw dual-wavelength buffer, no consumer yet), and both ACC streams. A threshold is only meaningful above the LEGITIMATE behaviour's characteristic scale, and that scale is known for exactly one of the five. | The ring figures come from a peer's plant-verified detectors on the real 04:53 (843,032 samples, fs 127.76) and 09-02 (2,320,376) files, run-length percentiles p50=1 p90=4 p99=14 p99.9=31 p99.99=48. No equivalent run-length distribution exists for Verity `_PPG.txt`, `_PPG2W.txt` or either ACC stream. The risk is asymmetric and mild in the safe direction: too HIGH a threshold under-reports (spans are missed, nothing false is published), and the per-channel hold classifier already absorbs the ACC case by shape rather than by threshold. Same query as the ring's, per stream, closes this — the corpus holds Verity `_PPG.txt` from the trio nights | OPEN |
| 2026-09-06-update-log-prints-marker-as-daemon | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/tepna-update.sh` | The deploy tick prints the DEPLOY MARKER under the label "the daemon is on", and the two are not the same thing once a marker can advance without a restart. `tepna-update.sh:263` sources `running_sha` from `$DEPLOYED_MARK`, and the `marker advanced, no restart` branch at :310 writes `$after` — the DISK sha — whenever the `capture-host/` delta is empty, while the daemon keeps running its older commit. The comment at :254 asserts the identity outright ("records the SHA the daemon is actually running"), so the wrong meaning is documented as well as printed. **The restart DECISION is unaffected and that is provable, not assumed**: the marker only advances without a restart when the `capture-host/` delta is empty, so the marker and the true running sha are always capture-host-code-identical and the delta driving `restart_owed` is the same computed from either. So the marker's honest meaning is "a sha whose capture-host code the daemon is running" — code-equivalent, never identity-equal — and the defect is that one line and one comment both claim identity. Cost is diagnostic, not operational: an operator comparing the tick against `/api/version` sees two authorities disagree with no way to tell which is lying, which is exactly the hierarchy Heron's three levels exist to keep straight. Remedy is wording plus an honest second number, not logic — print the marker AND `/api/version`, or say "the daemon's capture-host code is at" | Measured on the box 2026-09-06 across a real deploy. The 22:12:39 tick logged `restart still OWED from an earlier tick — the daemon is on 6669ce804704, disk is at f157b0035339` while `curl 127.0.0.1:8760/api/version` at 22:07:58 returned `{"git": "3d8eb5be", "dirty": false}` — a 2-commit disagreement, opened by the 20:04:34 and 21:07:50 ticks which both logged `marker advanced, no restart`. Code-equivalence confirmed by `git diff --name-only` over `capture-host/`: `3d8eb5be..6669ce80` = **0** files, `3d8eb5be..f157b003` = **4**, `6669ce80..f157b003` = **4** — same delta from the real sha and from the marker, hence the same restart verdict. Post-restart all three levels converged at `f157b003` with `ActiveEnterTimestamp` 22:12:43 | fixed #2751 |
| 2026-09-06-accraw-rate-overstated | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/capture.py` | The ring's `_ACCRAW.txt` record rate OVERSTATES its measurement rate by 6.4×. The ring updates ACC at 1.5625 Hz and the capture path writes a 10 Hz record stream, so ~84 % of records (5.4 of every 6.4) are a zero-order hold of the previous value rather than a new measurement. This is the §7 DRAWN-AXIS shape, not the §∅ absence shape — the held value is real data at a real, lower rate — so any consumer computing a rate, a span or an epoch grid from the record count is 6.4× wrong, and a run-length rule keyed on constant runs would flag 99.8 % of the file as fabricated absence and convict the device working as designed. | Three sessions, two nights (2026-09-04 ×2, 2026-09-05): record rate 9.979–10.000 Hz, distinct-value rate 1.562–1.565 Hz, records per distinct value 6.387–6.396 (= 32/5). Frame size is 5 records in 98.9 % of frames with a 500 ms median push interval, while repeat runs are 6–7 long and cross push boundaries 98.8–99.7 % of the time — which refutes both a per-push resend (would give run length 5, never crossing) and decimation (would need the true rate ABOVE the record rate, not below). Run-length histogram 6:18644 7:12057, i.e. 99.8 % of runs on two adjacent lengths. | fixed #2324 |
| 2026-09-06-raw-opcode-ns-column-is-literal-zero | 2026-09-06 | `capture-host/capture.py` | Three O2Ring raw-buffer writers pass a literal `0` for `sensor_ns`, so the `sensor timestamp [ns]` COLUMN reads as the instant zero rather than as absent — §∅ exactly, in a timestamp. The reasoning at each site is CORRECT and only the encoding is wrong: these opcodes carry no device clock, and the comment at :4213 says so ("Back-timing the block would be inventing per-sample instants the device never reported"), which is the right refusal. But `0` is in-band for a ns counter, so a consumer cannot distinguish "the device reported 0" from "the device reported nothing", and a reader that trusts the column places every one of these rows at the epoch. It is NOT confined to the new pletha stream — `ppg2w` and `accraw` have been writing it for as long as they have existed, so any night carrying those streams has the same column. ⚠️ The remedy needs BOTH halves and the writer is the half that is easy to miss: `writers.py write_pletha` interpolates `f"…;{sensor_ns};…"`, so passing `None` from the caller emits the literal string `None` into the file rather than an empty field. Fix is an empty field plus an explicit `timingSource: host` on these streams, per the Clock Contract §7 drawn-vs-measured rule — a stream with no device clock must be spendable as host-timed only, never as a second clock | Measured on tonight's real capture, device `S8AW2100`, session `20260906233028`, counting rows whose ns column is exactly `0`: **ACCRAW 15120/15120 · PLETHA 583/583 · PPG2W 303109/303109 — 100 % of all three, 318812 rows in one session**. Contrast on the same night proves it is not a device-wide absence: `PPG.txt` 1/190100 and Polar H10 `ECG.txt` 0/214693 both carry real per-sample ns. Call sites `capture.py:4198` (`write_acc(_aph, 0, 0.0, …)`), `:4216` (`write_pletha(_ph, 0, …)`), `:4235` (`write_ppg2w(ph, 0, …)`), against `:4293` `write_ppg(ph, ns_of[i], …)` which passes the real value. Found while watching the first-ever pletha capture 2026-09-06 | fixed #2324 |
| 2026-09-06-marker-isolation-heuristic-unvalidated | 2026-09-06 | `ppgdex-dsp.js` | Every span boundary `pinnedSpans` emits rests on an UNVALIDATED heuristic. An isolated `156` is treated as a documented inserted row and stepped over when merging (`ppgdex-dsp.js`, the annotation block), where "isolated" means a 156 whose immediate neighbours are not 156. That rule decides whether a marker-split plateau is ONE span or two, and a wrong answer changes the span count by ~22 % (Wren, corpus-wide: 2,533 of 11,325 raw zero runs are one event split by a marker). The heuristic has never been scored against its own failure mode — a RUN of 156s that is signal rather than markers, which isolation deliberately does not strip. `_PPG.txt` cannot score it: the marker is IN-BAND there, so the file offers no independent answer. The O2Ring `0x03` pletha opcode does carry the fiducial OUT-OF-BAND as a `beat` column, which is the first available oracle | ⚠️ THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT ORACLE WAS VACUOUS, and that is the finding. Measured 2026-09-06 (Wren, 552 rows / 11 markers on S8AW2100): lone-156-with-beat==0 → 0; **156 occurring in a RUN → 0**; run-156-with-beat==1 → 0; beat==1 on a non-156 → 0. Agreement is 11/11 and licenses NOTHING — the discriminating case never occurred, because the file contains no run of 156s at all, so the heuristic and the device agree only where agreement is trivial. A pass here is indistinguishable from a blind check. The test that would actually score it: a deliberate capture with pletha ENABLED and vitals OFF, so `0x03` runs at its own ~125 Hz rather than ~0.4 Hz, every marker carries a `beat`, and runs of 156 occur often enough to exercise the failure mode | fixed #2758 |
| 2026-09-07-drain-stamp-asserted-a-count-it-never-read | 2026-09-07 | `MUTATION-COVERAGE-SELECTION-2026-08-14-BRIEF.md` | My own 2026-09-02 triage stamp on this brief asserted `cpapdex`/`glucodex`/`hrvdex`/`motiondex` sit at **zero** equivalence-ledger entries and sized the remainder as "sweep those nodes to non-zero entries — one work-unit". They carry **26 / 48 / 71 / 100**. The stamp did not go stale: it was wrong when written, and the count was carried forward from an earlier reading rather than re-read at stamp time. Because a triage stamp is evidence-shaped, the next session spent it as measured — a second session was queued to execute the sweep and would have regenerated 245 already-committed entries before anyone re-read the file. The generalisable defect is PROCEDURAL: TRIAGE STAMPS THE BRIEF requires writing the verified state, and nothing verifies that the state written was re-measured rather than recalled. | Measured 2026-09-07 on `tools/mutate-equivalence.json`: 26/48/71/100. `git log --since=2026-08-25 -- tools/mutate-equivalence.json` is EMPTY, and reading the file at the 2026-09-02 commit gives the identical four counts, so the entries predate the stamp. Retracted in the brief header by the session that wrote it. Candidate mechanisms, none built and none obviously right: a stamp that cites a command whose output can be re-run (cheap, unenforced); a docs-ledger check that a stamp asserting a COUNT names the query producing it (narrow, and a wrong query still passes); or accept it as unmechanisable and rely on the retraction being cheap. Note the sibling class already logged as `2026-09-06-brief-header-file-line-citations-rot` — same root, different artefact | OPEN |

So the weaker class excused and the stronger did not: such an entry stayed in the distinguishable denominator, and `classifySurvivors`' terminal `else out.realGap.push(e)` reported it AS A REAL GAP — a TODO to write a test killing a mutant whose entry carries a proof that no such test exists. The 2026-08-19 pass that upgraded entries to `equivalent` with their proofs therefore made them weaker in effect. The class was also absent from the ledger's own `_README` vocabulary, so the data carried a class the code did not know and the docs did not define. | 4 entries affected: `hrvdex-dsp.js` 755 + 1096, `pulsedex-dsp.js` 448, `ecgdex-dsp.js` 1283. Fixed by adding `equivalent` to EXCUSING and to the `_README` vocabulary; selftest pins BOTH directions (excused, and not realGap) and is plant-verified — reverting EXCUSING reds both with `got 0 want 1`. ⚠️ The reusable part: the defect put the entry in a PLAUSIBLE bucket rather than dropping it, so every output looked well-formed. | 2026-09-07-drain-collides-with-predecessor-pull | 2026-09-07 | `capture-host/capture.py` | The auto-pull's follow-up drain is issued in the same second its own predecessor released the link, and BlueZ refuses it — so the remaining fragments are left on flash rather than collected. The primary `which=latest` pull completes and is correctly recorded; the immediately-following `which=new` drain then raises `org.bluez.Error.InProgress` because the controller has not finished tearing the previous operation down. The failure is handled honestly — it logs and leaves the data for the hourly poller, and the primary pull is not retracted — so nothing is lost permanently, but the drain never succeeds on the doff path and the fragments wait for a poller lap that may be an hour away. ⚠️ NOT the same defect as the in-process `offline_lock`, which is doing its job here: both calls are inside ONE process and one slot, so the lock sees no contention at all — the contention is BELOW it, in BlueZ, between an operation that has returned to Python and one that has not finished at the controller. A fix therefore cannot be another lock at the Python layer; it needs either a settle between the two pulls or the drain folded into the primary transaction | Measured on the box 2026-09-07 from the live journal, all three lines inside the same second: `05:43:47 stored-session pull finished — resuming live capture` · `05:43:47 auto-pull (not-worn): Wellue O2Ring-S → 1 new file(s)` · `05:43:47 pulling stored session (which=new)` · `05:43:47 auto-pull (not-worn): drain of the remaining fragments did not complete (link error: BleakDBusError('org.bluez.Error.InProgress', 'Operation already in progress')) — they stay on flash for the poller`. The primary pull that preceded it took 27 s (05:43:20 → 05:43:47) and succeeded | fixed #2768 |

The ones that matter: `**kw` dropped from `BleakClient(...)` survives, so the adapter pin the PR names as its operational finding can vanish and no test sees it (`test_no_bare_bleak_adapter_kwarg` pins the FORM of the kwarg, not that it is PASSED); `response=False` → `True` on the LIST write survives; `q.put_nowait(r)` → `None` survives, so a dropped reply reads as the "silence" finding; `auth_frame`/`setup_frame` argument mutants survive; `--family` `choices`/`default` removal survives. | 2026-09-07-bus-active-never-cleared-on-o2ring-disconnect | 2026-09-07 | `capture-host/telemetry.py` | `TelemetryBus._active` means "produced data at some point in THIS PROCESS" and nothing retracts it when a device disconnects, so any consumer that reads it as a liveness signal is wrong for as long as the daemon stays up. The flag's own comment is accurate and the meaning is defensible; the hazard is that "active" READS as "live" and the O2Ring is the device most often disconnected, so its streams are the ones that sit stale longest. Already misread once — `monitor.html`'s heading claimed live streams from it. The remedy is NOT simply to clear it on disconnect: `active` also gates which cards the Overview renders, and a stalled stream is deliberately still shown rather than vanishing, so clearing it would silently change the card set as well as any count. Whoever picks this up should decide between renaming it to say what it means (`_ever_pushed`) and giving the bus a separate, honestly-derived liveness field; the field that already answers liveness correctly is `health`, recomputed from the age of the last sample | Measured on the box 2026-09-07 with the O2Ring disconnected since 09:49 and only link errors in the journal since: `/api/state` reported `motion_o2` and `pi_o2` as `active=True, health='stall'`, and `/api/stream/motion_o2` served a buffer of zeros. The RUN is handled correctly — the parent sees `code !== 0` (a SIGABRT arrives as `code = null`), counts it `failed`, prints `✗ child exit null`, and leaves the night UNSTAMPED so the next fold redoes it (`tools/trio-batch.mjs` ~1085–1099). What survives is the on-disk residue: a reader that lists the night directory rather than checking the stamp sees a night carrying fewer nodes than it should, with nothing in the directory saying why. Measured on 2026-08-06 at a 1024 MB child heap: 2 of 5 exports written, both valid JSON, no marker. ⚠️ An earlier framing of this row said the fold 'does not fail loudly' — that was WRONG and is retracted here: the failure is loud at the parent, and only the artifacts are silent. | Heap sweep 2026-09-07, one night per run inside an 8 GB cgroup with swap off: 384 MB → 0/5 exports, 1024 → 2/5, both `FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit` + signal 6; 1536/2048/3072/4096/8192 → 5/5. The abort is invisible in `/usr/bin/time -v`'s `Exit status: 0` line, which sits directly under `Command terminated by signal 6` — reading the former is how the aborts were first mistaken for fast successes. Remedy is a hypothesis, not a decision: write the exports to a temp name and rename on a clean node completion, or drop a `.partial` marker the reader can see. Not built — the stamp already protects the FOLD, so this is only about a directory-listing consumer, and no such consumer has been shown to exist. | fixed #2420 |
| 2026-09-07-mutate-scratch-reuse-ignores-siblings | 2026-09-07 | `capture-host/tools/mutate.py` | The local mutation scratch is reused on a key that cannot see the change that invalidates it, and the result is a verdict that can be wrong in EITHER direction. `mutate.py` names the reusable directory `mut-<module>-<sha256 of the mutated module>[:12]`, so the cache key is the mutated module ALONE — but the scratch also holds every sibling a test reads FROM DISK, and the reuse path refreshes only `tests/` (wholesale, deliberately, after the `tests/_srcscan.py` incident its own comment records). Change a shell script, a fixture or a data file WITHOUT touching the mutated module and the hash does not move: the run executes your NEW tests against the OLD sibling. Measured 2026-09-07 on `night_report.py` — three consecutive runs reported a baseline failure I had already fixed, byte-identical each time including the temp path, because `tepna-report.sh` in the scratch still had mtime 20:56 while my fix was on disk. ⚠️ The same staleness inverts: a scratch holding an OLD sibling that happens to pass would report mutants KILLED that the current tree does not kill. The initial copy is complete (the `ignore` set is a denylist, and its comment explains why), so this is LOCAL-ONLY — a CI runner has no prior scratch and always takes the full-copy branch. Remedy is a hypothesis, not a decision: either widen the hash to cover the copied tree, or refresh non-module files on reuse the way `tests/` already is; the second keeps the cache's whole point (not regenerating a 100 MB mutant file) and costs one 7.7 MB copy | Reproduced 2026-09-07: `grep -c TEPNA_PYTHON` returned 3 in the scratch's `tests/test_tepna_report_sh.py` (refreshed) and **0** in its `tepna-report.sh` (stale) while the working tree had 1; `rm -rf /tmp/mut-night_report-*` and re-running took the same tree from a refusal (`REFUSING — 7 glob(s) recorded 0 tested mutants`) to a real survivor list. Scratch naming at `tools/mutate.py:302-304`, the reuse branch and its tests-only refresh at :330-342, the complete initial copy at :306-316 | fixed #2358 |
| 2026-09-08-wt-done-idle-check-counts-its-caller | 2026-09-08 | `tools/wt-done.mjs` | The IN-USE check (#2321) excludes only the scanner's own pid (`usersOfPath`, `if (Number(pid) === self) continue`) and its header states the `/proc` readlink form "cannot self-match". It cannot match the SCANNER; it does match the scanner's CALLER — a shell that has `cd`'d into the worktree to run `node tools/wt-done.mjs <path>` from inside it has `/proc/<pid>/cwd` under the target and is reported as a user of the tree, so the removal is refused. Third self-match shape of the night, one process up from the two CLAUDE.md §4 records: a path grep matches its own argv; a cwd scan matches its own caller. The refusal fails SAFE (a tree is kept, never lost), which is why this is a row and not a defect that blocks anyone — but the header claim is wrong as written and the tool gives no hint that the fix is to run it from the repo root. Remedy: exclude `process.pid` AND its ancestor chain (walk `/proc/<pid>/stat` ppid), or refuse up front with a named reason when `process.cwd()` is under the target — either makes the refusal say what it means. | Measured by Osprey 2026-09-08 on a merged worktree: `IN USE by PID 20773 … PID 20781 (tail -1)` — both the calling shell and its pipeline child, from inside the tree; the identical command from the repo root removed it cleanly. Exclusion at `tools/wt-done.mjs:75`, the "cannot self-match" claim at :58. | fixed #2402 |
