| 2026-09-10-cpap-unbonded-reason-was-wrong | 2026-09-10 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **CORRECTS `2026-09-06-cpap-pin-names-unbonded-adapter`, whose REMEDY was right and whose REASON was wrong — and the wrong reason is what hid a remedy costing one config line.** That row states *"The AS11's proprietary service needs an encrypted link, so `BleakCharacteristicNotFoundError` on `a6220003` is the EXPECTED result of an unbonded connection rather than a fault"*. Falsified four ways: (1) #2365's retry — a second connect on the **same unbonded adapter** — succeeded **97 of 98 times**, which is impossible if the link lacked a capability the service requires; (2) `[GATT] Cache = always` on vigil eliminated the failures with **no bond at all**, and D-Bus still reads `Paired=false Bonded=false` on that adapter today; (3) #2372's snapshot shows the **vendor service ABSENT from the D-Bus object tree** while the link read `connected` — an encryption failure presents as a characteristic present but unreadable, not as a whole service missing from a mirror; (4) btmon showed the ATT discovery COMPLETE including the CCCD at `0x0023`. The real mechanism is a publish race: BlueZ gates its GATT cache on `gatt_cache_is_enabled() = device_is_paired()`, so an unbonded device's object tree is torn down at every disconnect and re-exported on the next connect, and bleak snapshots at `ServicesResolved` before the export finishes. **Bonding WOULD have worked — as a side effect of enabling the cache, not because encryption was needed** — and that distinction is the whole cost of the wrong reason: while the failure was believed to be an encryption prerequisite, the one-line cache setting was not visible as an option, and an owner-attended pairing was the only remedy anyone could see. ⚠️ **DO NOT BOND NOW.** The condition the original row names still literally holds (the pin still names an adapter on which the AS11 is unbonded) and is now harmless; bonding would change a second variable on a box where the first one is measured and working. The original row's two withdrawn attributions stand withdrawn and are not re-derived here | Measured 2026-09-09/10 on vigil. `Cache = always` applied 21:18:31 (owner); across the next ~264 shadow polls at the daemon's stated 30 s cadence, **zero `link at failure` events and zero failovers**, against a baseline of **7 failures in ~64 polls (10.9 %/poll)** measured 20:46:42–21:18:31 the same evening — p ≈ 10⁻¹¹ under the baseline rate. Retry outcome 97/98 and the seven snapshot events (six byte-identical `00001801-…[]`, GAP service alone, no characteristics) are #2372's instrument, read on 2026-09-09. Code-side sibling fix landed as #2373 for hosts that do not carry the box's config | fixed #2376 |
| 2026-09-10-ring-connect-yield-is-not-a-failure-rate | 2026-09-10 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **REFINES `2026-09-06-ring-connect-attempts-mostly-fail`. Its headline ratio is not interpretable as a failure rate, and the aggregate flatters a per-night distribution that is far worse.** Three corrections, all from re-measurement. (a) **The statistic does not discriminate.** A `disconnected → connecting` attempt made while the ring is on its charger, out of range or in a drawer is a poll finding nothing, and it SHOULD fail; the ring is worn ~6–7 h in a 24 h day while the daemon attempts around the clock. So a low yield is exactly what a healthy system predicts, and "three of every four attempts never reach a session" reads as a defect while being consistent with no defect at all — the shape this ledger already names as a statistic both hypotheses predict. (b) **The aggregate is carried by two outliers.** Per-night yields over 18 nights: 0.0 1.6 2.1 2.3 2.7 4.8 5.7 6.5 7.2 9.1 9.5 10.0 10.5 11.1 13.8 14.3 **49.1 100.0** — median **8.2 %**, aggregate **28.2 %**. The two right-hand nights (629 attempts at 49.1 %, and 39/39 at 100.0 %) pull the mean to roughly 3.5× the typical night, so the original 31.5 % understates how bad an ordinary night looks and simultaneously cannot say whether that matters, per (a). (c) **The `failure` column is populated ZERO times on the link axis**, not once — 3 rows across all axes in 18 files, none of them link. So the classification that would make (a) answerable does not exist in the data at all. **Consequence for the unit:** the first work is NOT a gauge reporting this ratio — publishing an uninterpretable number on a status surface is worse than publishing none. It is the `failure` classification (device-not-present vs. auth/setup failure vs. contended adapter), after which "attempts that should have succeeded" becomes a denominator worth reporting. ⚠️ Also note `O2RING-POWER-AWARE-BLE-LIFECYCLE-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md` landed mid-window and bounds attempts (3 strikes → 1800 s cooldown): attempts/night fall from ~50–130 before it to ~20–45 after, with yield unchanged — which is the backoff working as designed and must not be read as a yield regression | Re-measured on vigil 2026-09-10 over **18 `OXYLIFE.csv` files** (2026-08-24 → 09-10), link axis only. ⚠️ The file is **semicolon-delimited** with a `# device=` preamble and carries a SECOND `power` axis (`pw_*` states, field 9 = `power`) in the same file; counting both axes double-counts the denominator, and a first pass that split on commas matched nothing at all. Link-axis totals `$4=="connecting"` → **1389 attempts**, `$4=="connected"` → **392 sessions** = 28.2 %; `$8!=""` → **0** populated failure cells on the link axis, 3 across all axes. Transition vocabulary confirmed by enumeration rather than assumed: `disconnected → connecting [scan + connect]` 1328, `connecting → disconnected [session ended]` 997, `connecting → connected [auth + setup]` 392, `paused_for_pull → connecting` 43. NOT measured, and each is a plausible discriminator for (a): yield by hour-of-day, by ring battery, by adapter, or against the worn window | OPEN |
| 2026-09-10-ring-fileset-fragmentation-decayed | 2026-09-10 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **CLOSES `2026-09-06-ring-fileset-never-resumes` WITHOUT BUILDING IT.** That row ends by instructing whoever sizes the work to *"re-measure over more post-hold nights before sizing"* — its post-deploy basis was **n = 25 gaps**. Re-measured 2026-09-10 with five further post-hold nights, and the projection collapses: **pre-hold 358 gaps, median 5 s, 96 % inside the 300 s window, 371 → 29 sets (12.8x); post-hold 15 gaps, median 794 s, 40 % inside, 20 → 14 sets (1.4x)** — six gaps across five nights is the entire prize. ⚠️ **"Fewer sets" was checked against the hypothesis that would make it BAD NEWS** — fewer sets because less was captured — and it fails: PPG volume holds (median 121 MB post vs 128.5 pre) and captured span is **6.45 h/night**, matching the ring independently measured at 5.4–6.8 h WORN (Heron, 2026-09-09). The ring now records essentially all of its worn time in 2–3 long sets a night, which is where the Verity and H10 already sat and is the target the original row aimed at. **The build is refused on the TRADE, not the difficulty:** the original row names two traps — three writers (`Spo2CsvWriter`, `OxyFrameLogWriter`, `RingClockLogWriter`) that open `"w"` and would TRUNCATE what they resume onto, and an `O2PpgGrid` that IS the timestamp column because the ring has no device clock, so resuming without carrying it puts two overlapping synthesized timelines in one file, *"a Clock-Contract fabrication, strictly worse than the fragmentation"*. Six gaps does not buy that risk. ⚠️ **CLOSED BY DECAY, NOT BY A FIX** — no PR repaired this, the conditions moved, and the state vocabulary has no word for that (`OPEN` would invite exactly the build being refused). **TRIPWIRE, so the decision is falsifiable rather than permanent: if signal-carrying ring sets/night returns above ~8 with a median inter-set gap under 300 s, the 12.8x regime is back and this must be re-derived from fresh numbers, not from these.** ⚠️ Do NOT re-derive the headline from lifetime totals — the original row warns that 82 % of all-time sets predate #1532, and this adds a second stratum: everything before the 2026-09-05 storm hold is a different regime again | Measured on vigil 2026-09-10 over 18 nights (2026-08-23 → 09-10), signal-carrying sets only, defined as `*_PPG.txt` > 10 kB. Gap = next set filename stamp minus previous set `PPG.txt` mtime. Strata split at the storm hold deploy 2026-09-05 09:05 UTC, with 09-05 excluded as straddling and 09-10 excluded as an incomplete night in progress (0.6 MB when read). Post-hold gaps in seconds: 09-06 `[4428, 0, 0, 794, 56393]` · 09-07 `[2653, 22, 3245, 449, 60, 42, 51222]` · 09-08 `[82, 67292]` · 09-09 `[60568]` — the large values are between-wear day gaps that SHOULD separate sets, and only 6 of 15 fall inside the window. The two outlier nights the hold now prevents are in the same table: 2026-08-29 at 305 signal sets, 2026-09-05 at 40 | fixed #2378 |
| 2026-09-10-stale-brief-guard-reads-the-root-base | 2026-09-10 | `.claude/hooks/guard-stale-brief.sh` | The stale-brief guard computes "your branch base" from the SHARED ROOT checkout rather than from the tree the edit is happening in, so it fires on a worktree that already contains every commit it names. The failure is SAFE (it refuses, never overwrites) and the documented hatch works, but it teaches sessions to reach for `CLAUDE_ALLOW_STALE_BRIEF=1` reflexively — which is the habit that makes the guard useless the day it fires for a real reason. Remedy is a hypothesis, not a decision: resolve the base from the edited file's own worktree (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` on the target path) rather than from `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, or compare against the branch's actual merge-base rather than the root's HEAD | Reproduced 2026-09-10 in a fresh worktree at `6085943f`: `git log --oneline -3 -- briefs/RESIDUE.md` in that tree returns `8d8f7d89`, `3e4a4117`, `f70e3004` — three of the commits the hook listed as missing — while the hook's message names the base it used, `078998e7`, which is the shared root's HEAD and not the branch's. The root measured **32** commits behind `origin/main` at that moment (`git -C <root> rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main`); an earlier draft of this row asserted 78 from memory and was corrected by running the command | fixed #2380 |
| 2026-09-10-guard-row-generalised-a-narrow-cause | 2026-09-10 | `.claude/hooks/guard-stale-brief.sh` | **CORRECTS `2026-09-10-stale-brief-guard-reads-the-root-base`, filed by me earlier the same day, whose stated mechanism is FALSE.** That row says the guard *"computes your branch base from the SHARED ROOT rather than from the tree the edit is happening in"* — which is the general defect `STALE-BRIEF-GUARD-MEASURES-THE-WRONG-TREE-2026-08-18-BRIEF.md` diagnosed and **fixed on 2026-08-18**. The hook resolves `edit_dir` from `dirname(file_path)` on the Edit/Write route and from the first `cd` in the command on the Bash route, and both work: verified 2026-09-10 by editing a guarded path from an existing current worktree — ALLOWED. **The true condition is much narrower: the `cd` target must not EXIST YET.** A command that creates its worktree and edits a guarded file in the same invocation (`git worktree add <p> && cd <p> && cat >> briefs/X.md`) reaches the hook before `<p>` exists, so `[ -d "$cd_dir" ]` fails, `edit_dir` falls back to the hook's cwd, and the base becomes the shared root's HEAD. Both of the morning's blocks had exactly that shape, and the generalisation went from one observation to a claim about the guard as a whole. ⚠️ **HOW IT HAPPENED IS THE REUSABLE PART: I did not run the mandated `tools/doc-search.mjs` before filing.** CLAUDE.md §📌 makes a semantic search step 2 of any pickup precisely because grep finds only your own vocabulary; the first query run afterwards returned that DONE brief as its second hit. Four of five units that day skipped the step, and this is the one where it cost something — a merged row asserting a fixed defect is still broken, which is worse than no row because it sends a picker-up to re-fix working code. **The narrow cause is NOT silently repairable** — at PreToolUse time there is no tree to ask, so any base would be a guess — and it fails CLOSED, the safe direction. What was fixed instead is the MESSAGE: the denial now names the missing tree and says the base is the root's, because a block that cannot explain itself trains the reader to reach for `CLAUDE_ALLOW_STALE_BRIEF=1` by reflex, and a hatch-by-reflex guard is the one that fails the day it is right. The practice fix costs nothing: create the worktree in its own call, then edit from it | Reproduced 2026-09-10 in both directions. DENIED: `git worktree add -q <p> origin/main && cd <p> && echo x >> briefs/RESIDUE.md` from a worktree freshly made off `origin/main`, the message naming base `078998e7` — the shared root's HEAD, measured 33 commits behind at that moment — and listing ten commits the new worktree already contained. ALLOWED: the same guarded path edited from a worktree that already existed. Hook logic read end to end at `.claude/hooks/guard-stale-brief.sh` (the `edit_dir` block and the `cd`-extraction that follows it). Three self-test legs added and seen to fail first: the missing-tree denial, the message naming it, and an anti-vacuity leg asserting an EXISTING tree carries no such note — without the last one the explanation could become wallpaper on every denial and stop meaning anything | fixed #2380 |
| 2026-09-10-fsync-band-crossed-and-the-remedy-is-narrower | 2026-09-10 | `capture-host/writers.py` | **The pre-stated band of `2026-09-05-fsync-on-loop-unmeasured` is CROSSED, and its named remedy is the wrong shape.** That row set the decision before the data: *"`fsync_max_ms` ≥ 250 ms on any live stream over one real night ⇒ move the writer off-loop; < 50 ms every night for a week ⇒ close this row as not-a-defect"*. Measured 2026-09-10 over the six nights since the instrument shipped: **20 `SLOW fsync` events, on every one of the six nights (1·2·2·7·5·3), 252–506 ms with one at 1702 ms**, spread 01:06–21:45 rather than clustered — so it is recurring behaviour, not one bad moment, and the ≥250 ms arm is met many times over. ⚠️ **BUT THE REMEDY NAMED IN THAT ROW — "a thread or queue writer" — CARRIES A COST IT DOES NOT NEED TO.** The row declined to build it because moving the WRITER off-loop *"changes the P0 path from direct-write to queued-write and introduces a loss class the current code does not have (rows in a queue at crash)"*. That objection is real for a queued writer and **does not apply to the actual defect**: `flush()` is `self._fh.flush()` (userspace → kernel page cache, cheap) and only `self._health.fsync()` (kernel → disk) is the 250–1702 ms part. After `flush()` the rows are already in the kernel, so a **process** crash loses nothing — only a power cut inside the un-fsynced window does, which is the window that exists today. **Move the fsync, not the writer, and the loss class never appears.** Shape that fits the house pattern: `_maybe_flush` keeps `flush()` on-loop and stops calling fsync; a periodic supervisor task fsyncs the live writers through `asyncio.to_thread` (the established off-loop idiom here — `capture.py` uses it for tree walks, QC and rebinds). No new thread to own, no queue, no rows in flight. ⚠️ **NOT BUILT HERE, deliberately:** it is the P0 write path on a box recording irreplaceable nights, it needs a live-writer registry (see `2026-09-06-writer-close-list-hand-kept`, which is the same enumeration problem), and the `SLOW fsync` warning text — *"on the event loop"* — becomes false the moment it lands and must move with it. The band authorised a remedy, not a rushed one | Measured on vigil 2026-09-10, `journalctl -u tepna-capture --since 2026-09-05`, counting the once-per-file `SLOW fsync` warning (`writers.py` `_FlushHealth.fsync`, `SLOW_FSYNC_MS = 250.0`). Events per night Sep 05→10: 1, 2, 2, 7, 5, 3. Durations, sorted by time: 506, 268, 310, 414, 372, 254, 343, 367, 350, 353, 263, 300, 403, 289, 341, 252, **1702**, 284, 310, 282 ms. Streams affected include `Polar_H10_*_ECG.txt`, `Polar_H10_*_ACC.txt` and `Polar_VeritySense_*_PPI.txt` — i.e. live capture files, which is the arm the band names. ⚠️ Because the warning fires ONCE PER FILE, 20 events is a floor on the number of slow fsyncs, not a count of them; `fsync_max_ms` in STATUS is the per-writer maximum and was not sampled here. ⚠️ A disk-pressure confound was considered and is NOT excluded: `/srv/data` measured **98 % full (6.1 G free of 227 G)** the same day, and fsync latency on a near-full filesystem is a known effect — so freeing space may reduce the magnitude without touching the mechanism. That does not change the band's verdict (the wait is real and on the loop either way) but it does mean a post-fix measurement must record free space beside the latency, or an improvement from housekeeping will be read as the fix working | fixed #2382 |
| 2026-09-10-daemon-restarts-are-idle-gated | 2026-09-10 | `capture-host/tepna-update.sh` | **NARROWS `2026-09-06-capture-daemon-restarts-nightly`, whose COUNT is real and whose implied HARM is not.** That row says *"The capture daemon starts many times a night on a box that is never rebooted, and no artifact reports the count"* — true, and the count is high: 17 · 26 · 20 · 23 starts/day over 2026-09-04→07, falling to 3 · 7 · 5 over 09-08→10 (that fall is UNEXPLAINED and deliberately not fitted to a story). But the reason a restart count matters is that a restart drops the BLE links and opens a new file-set mid-night, and **that is not what happens**: measured over the last four nights, **9 restarts fell in capture hours (00:00–07:00) and 0 of them landed inside a live capture**. The deploy path gates on idleness — `tepna-update.sh` logs `box is idle — restarting the daemon` before calling the restart helper — and every one of the nine clustered at **05:10–06:54**, i.e. after the ~04:30 doff that `2026-09-10-…` (Heron's H10/ring joint-doff finding) places the wearer getting up. The mechanism intended to prevent this is working, and the row's number was being read as evidence of a harm nobody had checked for. ⚠️ **What remains TRUE from the original row is the bookkeeping half**: nothing reports the restart count, so the next person to suspect fragmentation has to re-derive it from `journalctl` as I did. That is worth a status field or a night-report line — but it is worth it as *observability*, not as a fix for a defect, and sizing it as the latter would repeat the error this row corrects. ⚠️ Stated limit on the negative: "live capture" is defined as a restart timestamp falling inside a signal-carrying `*_PPG.txt` set's span (filename stamp → mtime). A restart while the ring was WORN but the link was already down reads as "not capturing" — which is benign for this question (there is nothing to interrupt) but means the result bounds interruption of CAPTURE, not of WEAR | Measured on vigil 2026-09-10. Starts/day from `journalctl -u tepna-capture … \| grep -c "Started tepna-capture.service"`. Capture-hour restarts, all nine: 09-07 06:01:31 · 06:12:19 · 06:12:46 · 06:40:46 · 09-08 05:32:48 · 09-09 05:10:07 · 06:54:11 · 06:54:37 · 09-10 05:12:39, each tested against **77** signal-carrying sets across `/srv/tepna/captures/2026-09-*`; every one returned "ring not capturing". Stops are `Deactivated successfully`, i.e. deliberate restarts rather than crashes — no `Failed`/`Main process exited` in the same window. | 2026-09-11-withdrawn-rows-cannot-close | 2026-09-11 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | A row WITHDRAWN as a false positive has no reachable closing state, so the open count cannot converge. `docs-ledger` check8b fixes the vocabulary at exactly `OPEN` · `→ \`<NAME>-BRIEF.md\`` · `fixed #NNNN` (`tests/dex-tests.js`, `stateOk`), and all three assert something happened: a fix, or a promotion. A row whose defect turned out NOT TO EXIST can honestly claim neither — closing it `fixed #NNNN` asserts a repair that never occurred and cites a PR that did not make one, which is the fabricated-close analogue of the fabricated tier §🎫 forbids. The live instance is a stuck PAIR, not a hypothetical: `2026-09-06-docs-index-role-disagrees-with-brief-header` is withdrawn in terms by `2026-09-06-link-substring-is-not-a-row-match` ("which is a FALSE POSITIVE — there is no header/Role disagreement"), and BOTH are still OPEN — the withdrawn row because no state fits, the withdrawal itself because it is not a defect either. Every such correction therefore ADDS two permanently-open rows, so the queue grows with the ledger's own accuracy. Same shape for the other correction rows here (`…-ring-duration-counts-data-not-time` corrects `…-counter-bimodal`; `…-ring-connect-yield-is-not-a-failure-rate` refines `…-connect-attempts-mostly-fail`; `…-restart-row-marker-premise-absent` falsifies a remedy premise). ⚠️ This is a CONTRACT question, not a code fix: a fourth state (`withdrawn <key>`, resolving like `→`) needs the owner's call, and widening the vocabulary without one would let a genuine defect be closed by asserting it is not one — strictly worse than a queue that over-counts | Found 2026-09-11 while draining the ledger to the three prescribed outcomes, none of which has a branch for a withdrawal. A consumer comparing `times` to a ms timebase is off by 1000x, and the error is INVISIBLE to the assertion shape people actually write: #2413's own end-to-end selftest shipped that exact bug and PASSED, because it asserted only that both beat trains were non-empty. Every current consumer happens to handle it (qrs-yield-worker.js names its local `timesSec` and converts truth into seconds; ppi-match.mjs:181 documents the `peaks` unit by hand) — so this is a live trap with no live victim, and the hand-written unit note is itself evidence the surface is confusing. | 2026-09-12-partial-fold-reads-as-zero-overlap | 2026-09-12 | `tools/tch-multinight.mjs` | **REFINES `2026-09-07-partial-exports-survive-child-abort`, which said no directory-listing consumer had been shown to exist. But the feared harm does NOT occur and is retracted here: a night missing a TCH corner is REFUSED, not silently estimated, because `runNight` needs 12 aligned epochs and an absent corner aligns to zero. What is wrong is the DIAGNOSIS and the DENOMINATOR. The refusal prints `overlap 0 < 12`, which attributes an aborted fold to insufficient epoch overlap — a genuine `overlap 0` means the three nodes recorded disjoint windows, an unrelated and much more alarming condition. And the aborted night still counts in the corpus line (`all 2 cohorted night(s)`, `1 estimated / 2 nights`), so a run's estimation yield is degraded by a fold that never finished, with nothing on disk or in the output saying so. This is the case FOR the parent row's `.partial` marker over its temp-name-and-rename alternative: a marker is the only one of the two a reader can SEE and name. | Measured 2026-09-12 with two synthetic nights of 30 five-minute epochs under `--dir`: the 3-corner night estimates (σ 0.99/1.18/1.44 E/P/O) and the 2-corner night prints `night-partial  0   —    overlap 0 < 12` while the footer reads `all 2 cohorted night(s)` and `1 estimated / 2 nights`; neither directory contained a `.trio-stamp` and the tool never looked for one (`grep -c trio-stamp tools/tch-multinight.mjs` returns 0, as does the same grep for STAMP) | fixed #2424 |
| 2026-09-12-no-durable-capture-file-deletion-guard | 2026-09-12 | `#2418` | **The box has no durable guard against capture-file deletion.** Tonight's loss (a live ring session's `_SPO2.csv` and 800 KB `_ACCRAW.txt`, root cause fixed by #2418) was noticed only by an ad-hoc poll a peer session had armed in its own task state — committed nowhere, so when that session ends nothing watches, and the next occurrence is silent. Three properties of that watcher are the design constraints for a committed one: (1) it thresholds at >64 KB, so it caught ACCRAW and missed SPO2 — the primary vitals stream, routinely under 64 KB early in a session; (2) it polls, so a delete-and-recreate between two polls (exactly the resume path's close/unlink/reopen shape) never registers; (3) it watches the path from outside the box, so it cannot name the deleter — a guard inside the daemon can. Candidate: the end-of-night back-check already walks every file-set; a file that existed in an earlier `LINK.csv`/RUNS sidecar and is absent or smaller at close is the observable, and it needs no threshold. | `grep -rn 65536 capture-host/*.py capture-host/*.sh tools/*.mjs` matches only buffer sizes; no `capture-host` module or `tools/*.mjs` implements a capture-file loss check (peer-verified 2026-09-12, Heron); the #2418 deletions were reported by the peer's session-local Monitor `bmhpqgl9q`, not by any repo mechanism | withdrawn 2026-09-20-no-loss-guard-during-live-capture |

A wholly-invalid channel is filled with a hardcoded `97` (`firstValid = validLo === 40 ? 97 : 60`) — a fabricated saturation and a fabricated heart rate. During a hold the trace is FLAT, so no desaturation can be detected there and the rolling baseline is contaminated by a run that never happened; ODI-3/ODI-4 and every AHI estimate derived from them inherit it. The same function is used for the HR channel at (20, 240). ⚠️ This is NOT established as the cause of the measured ODI under-count — the SHHS hypopnea rule does not require a 4 % desaturation, so a definitional shortfall is expected too, and the detector's own sensitivity is unmeasured; three contributors are tangled and separating them is a different unit. | Measured 2026-09-12 over the first 15 SHHS1 records: mean 2.76 % of SaO₂ samples outside [40,100]; shhs1-200001 14.75 % invalid with a single held value spanning 3840 s (64 min), shhs1-200009 8.92 % / 2765 s, shhs1-200013 4.77 % / 1421 s. It already carries a `DRAWN AXIS` refusal keyed on `ax.timingSource === 'none'`, and every drawn stream reaches it as exactly one of two values: `'none'` when no host anchors were usable (refused), or `'host'` when they were. `'host'` is **deliberately admitted**, and `pat-gate.js`'s own comment says why — "the axis was drawn, but real host anchors then placed it on host time, so the two devices genuinely do sit on one timebase, which is the condition this gate exists to require"; refusing it "would cost the box nights that are the only ones with a second clock at all". 'host' : …` / `axisSynthetic ? 'none' : …`) and `ecgdex-dsp.js:4825`, which is what makes the `'none'`/`'host'` split exhaustive. `git log -S'quantizedShare >= 0.67' -- ppgdex-dsp.js` returns `e57185ec` (#2212, 2026-09-05). `grep -l 'data-inline-src="pat-gate.js"' *.html` returns nothing, and `ppgdex-dsp.js` is inlined by PpgDex + the two orchestrators — not by any `clock.js` bundle | OPEN |
| 2026-09-12-h10-rebond-waits-behind-psftp-sync | 2026-09-12 | `NRF52840-DONGLE-FLASHING-2026-09-07-BRIEF.md` | **On an adapter swap the H10's re-bond is gated behind five PS-FTP clock syncs that each need the bond it lacks — 14 min of a known-fixable link outage, and the bond-error path logs nothing.** `capture.py` checks `ensure_bonded` once at task start (warns on `False`; the `except` at ~2537 writes `last_error="bond error: …"` with NO log line), then runs `auto_sync_clock` (PS-FTP — encrypted link required, 120 s budget, up to 12 `NotConnected` retries ≈ 2 min) BEFORE `_connect` on every iteration, and re-checks the bond only at `iteration % _REBOND_EVERY(=5) == 0`. Bonds are per-adapter in BlueZ, so re-pinning `config.yaml` to a new controller leaves every H10 connect refusing the device's `SMP: Security Request` (`Pairing not supported 0x05`) and the daemon hanging up at 2 s (`0x16`) — five times, each preceded by a sync that cannot succeed. The fix shape: check `is_bonded` (or a `looks_like_a_stale_bond` tell — `NotConnected` after a 2 s local teardown is one) BEFORE the sync, and re-bond on the first miss rather than the fifth; and log the exception path. | Measured on vigil 2026-09-12: swap 21:36:53 → `BlueZ reports no bond — re-pairing (attempt 1/72)` 21:50:40 → `re-bonded` 21:51:03 → ECG streaming 21:51:05; btmon `probe-h10-hci0-20260912T214207.btsnoop` (4 cycles/60 s, handles 117→132, every disconnect reason `0x16`). The Verity was `Bonded: yes` on hci0 by 21:37 (its pairing exchange was not captured — only the H10 window was). | OPEN |

| 2026-09-13-kill-selfmatch-needs-no-process-tool | 2026-09-13 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **CORROBORATES `2026-09-05-pgrep-selfmatch-kills-too` with a fourth instance, on a different machine and in a form that uses NO process tool at all — which sharpens the class the parent asks §👥.4 to name.** The parent's three instances are `pkill -f`, `pgrep -f` in a `for`, and a `case` label; together they read as "a `pgrep`/`pkill` hazard". Mine used neither: a `/proc/*/cmdline` scan piped to `xargs kill`, on rig-x870 rather than vigil. It died the same way, exit 144 (128+SIGTERM), because the scanning shell's OWN command line contained the worktree path it was scanning for. So the invariant is not about `pgrep`, and a remedy phrased around process tools would miss this: **any list-then-act command whose own text contains the pattern it matches will act on itself**, whatever supplies the list. Same premise §👥.4 already states for the WAITING case, same different consequence the parent identified — immediate rather than eventual, destroying the acting process instead of hanging it. It also confirms the parent's proposed two-command discipline by the negative: the recovery was exactly that — list matching PIDs in one command, read them, kill numeric PIDs in a second command containing no pattern — and it worked first time. ⚠️ NOT a duplicate filing. The parent stays the row to execute; this exists because its evidence cell reads as vendor-specific, and a §👥.4 edit written from `pkill`/`pgrep` alone would leave the form that actually bit a session on a second machine uncovered. Remedy unchanged and still the owner's: a CLAUDE.md §👥.4 sentence | Measured 2026-09-12 ~20:05 UTC-4: a `ls /proc/*/cmdline`-driven loop matching a worktree path, piped to `xargs -r kill`, returned exit 144 and terminated the tool call before its kill list was applied — leaving a stale `npm run check` running against a tree that had since been edited, so the next verdict would have described the wrong tree. Restarting to a fresh log, with no kill at all, was the fix | OPEN |
| 2026-09-13-ppg2w-200hz-was-already-written-down | 2026-09-13 | `briefs/DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md` | **The ~200 Hz `ppg2w` fill rate measured in #2433 is not a new number — `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH` §5 has carried it since 2026-08-05, and `O2RING-RAW-DUAL-WAVELENGTH-FOLLOWUPS` §7.1 never took it.** §5's capture-configuration table gives `O2Ring RtPpg` the decision **ENABLE — 200 Hz**, with the rationale `cmd=0x05, payload {0x07,0x01}; [u16 count] then 9-byte records`, and its own bandwidth figure checks it internally: **1800 B/s ÷ 9 bytes per record = exactly 200 records/s** (the same line's 125 B/s ÷ 1 byte = the 125 Hz pleth). That is protocol-derived and independent of any recording. Meanwhile the raw-dual brief treated the `0x05` rate as its own §7.1 OPEN QUESTION and pre-stated decision bands of `[95,105] → 100 Hz` and `[118,132] → 125 Hz` — **neither of which contains 200** — and its §2.1a reasoned from `> 102 Hz` to "consistent with the parent brief's 125.000 (ADC) prediction". So two briefs disagreed about the same stream by a factor of 1.6-2, one of them holding the answer, and nobody reconciled them. #2433's corpus measurement (median 200.0-200.4 Hz across three post-double-drain nights, from unsaturated replies against the inter-reply arrival gap) is therefore an INDEPENDENT EMPIRICAL CONFIRMATION of the protocol figure rather than a surprise, which is a stronger result than the PR claims: it matches a number derived from the record layout, by a completely different route. ⚠️ What it still does not settle is §7.1's real question — whether a 9-byte record is one ADC sample per wavelength. 200 records/s carrying IR and RED each could be 200 Hz per wavelength or something else; the record layout says two u32 channels per record, which points at 200 Hz per wavelength, but that is reading a spec rather than measuring a device and is not cut here. The reusable part: a brief's OPEN QUESTION is not evidence that the answer is unknown to the repo. Both briefs were live, both were being worked, and the pre-stated bands were written without consulting the sibling that had the figure | Verified 2026-09-13 against `origin/main` at `d629ae25`: `DEVICE-RATE-TRUTH-2026-08-05-BRIEF.md:283` carries the 200 Hz row and the 1800 B/s figure; the raw-dual brief's §2.1a carries the `[95,105]`/`[118,132]` bands and the `> 102 Hz` bound. The arithmetic tying them (1800/9 = 200) is on the same line as the claim it checks | OPEN |
| 2026-09-13-odi-threshold-has-no-injection-point | 2026-09-13 | `oxydex-dsp.js` | **The shipped ODI threshold cannot be varied without editing source, so no tool can measure the shipped index under an alternative criterion.** `processNight` calls `detectDesatEvents(spo2, { dropPct: DexKernel.K.ODI_DROP, blArr })` and takes no override; `DexKernel.K` is FROZEN, so assigning `ODI_DROP` throws. That freeze is CORRECT for shipping — a clinical constant nobody can retune by accident — and this row is not a request to remove it. The consequence is the finding: criterion research must drive `detectDesatEvents` directly, which is NOT the shipped index. Measured 2026-09-13 over 99 SHHS1 records, the raw detector at the shipped parameter values yields a median 9.12 events/h over TST while the ODI-4 `processNight` publishes is 2.70 — a 3.38x gap, because the pipeline's gating, artifact handling and denominator remove roughly two thirds of raw events. A sweep over the raw detector therefore cannot be quoted against the shipped index, and one that was read as a 3.4 events/h improvement was measuring the absent pipeline (its chosen `minSec` of 20-25 s compensating for it). Remedy is a hypothesis, not a decision: an OPTIONAL params argument on `processNight` that defaults to the frozen constant would make the shipped path swept-able without unfreezing anything — but it widens a compute-path surface and is an owner call. | `ctx.DexKernel.K.ODI_DROP = 3` throws "Cannot assign to read only property"; `grep -n 'ODI_DROP' oxydex-dsp.js` shows the call sites take no override; both pinned by assertions in `tools/nsrr-criterion-sweep.mjs` so a refactor that unfreezes it surfaces there | OPEN |
| 2026-09-13-convention-measured-on-the-signal-subset | 2026-09-13 | `tools/nsrr-oxydex-odi.mjs` | **A cohort property was measured on the subset that happens to carry SIGNALS, and it was ~4 points off.** The SHHS1 desaturation-scoring convention was characterised as "a median 70.9 % of scored desaturations are shallower than 3 %" from the 99 records that have EDFs. But the annotation side is the FULL cohort — 5136 scored records, no signal file required — and the same statistic there is 66.7 % (median per-record), or 59.3 % pooled over 735880 events. The conclusion held; the number did not. ⚠️ The general defect is the habit, not this figure: a SCORING CONVENTION is a property of the cohort and its scorers, so measuring it on the signal-carrying subset inherits whatever selected those 99 records for distribution. Nothing about the analysis needed the signals — it is pure annotation parsing — so the narrow sample was chosen by convenience, not necessity. Other cohort properties in this lane may carry the same error: event-rate distributions, hypopnea/apnea ratios, arousal counts and stage fractions are all computable over 5136 and have so far been taken over 99 or fewer. | Measured 2026-09-13 over all 5136 annotation files: 735880 desaturation events, 0 records with none, depth min 0.0 / p25 1.0 / median 2.0 / p75 4.0 / p95 8.0 / max 59.0 %; share below 3 % = 59.3 % pooled and 66.7 % as a per-record median, against the 70.9 % first reported from 99 records | fixed #2442 |
| 2026-09-13-dst-fallback-splits-the-host-axis | 2026-09-13 | `capture-host/jitterfloor.py` | **Seven call sites convert the `Phone timestamp` column with `.timestamp()` on a NAIVE local-civil stamp, and on the DST fall-back night that call maps one wall-clock hour onto two different instants — so the host axis steps BACKWARDS mid-recording while the device counter marches on.** The two columns are anchored differently and both anchors are documented in the producer: `last_sensor_ns` counts from 2000-01-01 **UTC** (`capture.py:_utcnow` — "Device clocks are set in UTC ... so skew is measured against UTC"), while `Phone timestamp` is naive **local** civil time (`writers._phone_ts` — "Do not pass a UTC instant"; the write site at `capture.py:2748` passes `_now()`, not `_utcnow()`). Converting local→epoch is therefore CORRECT and required; what is unguarded is that the conversion is AMBIGUOUS for exactly one hour a year. Python resolves a naive fall-back stamp with `fold=0`, so the second pass through 01:00-02:00 is read as the first. `jitterfloor._parse_stamp_ms` justifies itself with "only deltas are consumed downstream, so the zone-free epoch is immaterial" — true for a CONSTANT offset and false at the seam, because the seam is not an offset, it is a step INSIDE the series. ⚠️ The consequence is not confined to a diagnostic: `nightqc.py:1960-1961` builds a night's `span` as `t1 - t0` from the same call, so a fall-back night's span reads ONE HOUR SHORT, and span is the denominator of `coverage = rows / (hz * span)` — coverage is INFLATED, which is the direction that hides a degraded stream rather than inventing one. The arrival path (`nightqc.py:1267`) gets a -3 600 000 ms discontinuity in `host_ms - device_ms`, splitting the population `clock_offset.estimate` fits into two clusters an hour apart. ⚠️ PROSPECTIVE, NOT RETROSPECTIVE, and stated that way on purpose: the mechanism is proven synthetically and the corpus contains NO crossing night (Jun-Sep 2026), so this is a demonstrated mechanism with a zero-night prevalence, not a measured defect rate. The box is `America/New_York` and records nightly, so the next occurrence is **2026-11-01** — about seven weeks from this row. Remedy is a design question and not cut here: the honest information (which pass, or the UTC offset in force) is ABSENT from the sidecar, so a consumer cannot recover it — which points at the WRITER recording the offset or stamping UTC, with consumers detecting a backwards step and refusing, rather than at seven independent patches | Measured 2026-09-13 under `TZ=America/New_York` on the box's own zone: `datetime.fromisoformat('2026-11-01T01:30:00.000').timestamp()` = 1793511000 against `fold=1` = 1793514600, a 3600 s ambiguity; a series crossing the seam reads 01:58 EDT = 1793512680, 01:59 EDT = 1793512740, then 01:00 EST = 1793509200 — a **step of -3540 s**. The seven sites: `jitterfloor.py:49` (`strptime(...).timestamp()`), `nightqc.py:1267`, `nightqc.py:1960`, `nightqc.py:1961`, `timeline.py:292`, `link_distress.py:301`, `tools/scan_coexistence.py:82` | fixed #2774 |
| 2026-09-13-folded-base-prefers-the-largest-candidate | 2026-09-13 | `capture-host/jitterfloor.py` | **`_folded_base` picks 2× the true base when about half the deltas are doubled gaps, and the cause is the mirror of the bug its own docstring cites.** The module says the score is RELATIVE because "an absolute argmin always hands the win to the smallest base" — but when the true base divides every candidate cleanly, each candidate leaves the SAME absolute residual, so dividing by `c` mechanically ranks the LARGEST candidate best. Reachable only since the lattice guard (#PENDING) started routing exactly-drawn axes to `folded`; before that this shape took the `vs-device` path. | Synthetic 1/3-drop fixture (`test_missed_frames_do_not_inflate_folded_jitter`, now `xfail(strict=True)`): median delta 999.0 because just over half the deltas ARE the 2× gaps, so the median lands ON the doubled cluster; candidate scores m=1 c=999.0 → 0.001001 (wins), m=2 c=499.5 → 0.002002 (true base), m=3 → 0.003003, m=4 → 0.004004 — exactly proportional to m, which is the tell. Base 999 makes every genuine 500 ms delta read as a 499 ms residual → jitter 248 ms vs the correct ~8 ms. ⚠️ Real-corpus impact is SMALL and must not be overstated: the O2Ring's true drop rate is ~2 %, where folded returns 10.5 ms (Sena) / 11.5 ms (Zephyr) — measured 2026-09-13 over 2026-09-11/09-12. This bites near a 1/3 drop rate, not at observed rates. | fixed #2450 |
| 2026-09-13-executing-session-stamps-nothing | 2026-09-13 | `CLAUDE.md` | **§📌 binds the session that TRIAGES a brief and nothing binds the session that EXECUTES one of its items, so work is routinely finished and left reading as outstanding.** §📌 is explicit for triage — "TRIAGE STAMPS THE BRIEF … a triage that leaves the header untouched has thrown away its own product" — and there is no counterpart for execution. The executing session lands the code, writes the changeset, and never returns to tick the box it just discharged. **FOUR instances in ONE sweep, 2026-09-13, all verified against the tree:** (1) `DOCS-LEDGER-HEADER-REFS-2026-08-27` — header DONE since 2026-08-27 and §4a records the execution, while the original `## Done when` block kept three unticked boxes, so a reader grepping `- [ ]` saw three open items on a finished brief. (2) `FINISHED-WORK-IMPROVEMENTS-2026-08-20` §B2 — shipped as `nightarchive.unarchived_nights`, wired at `capture.py:6275`, and the brief still prescribed building it. (3) the same brief's §B4 — landed 2026-09-03 in `49a774a6`, whose own code comment at `trio-batch.mjs:1521` reads "THE CROSS-CHECK B4 ASKS FOR"; the work and the brief were connected by that comment and by nothing else. (4) the same brief's §B3 — its adopt-or-decline was settled in a DIFFERENT brief (`O2RING-TIME-CAPABILITY-WIRING-2026-08-19-BRIEF.md:57`, "the RTC is declined as a corner, the fiducial network is the TCH direction") while §B3 still reads "Adopt or decline". ⚠️ THE COST IS PAID BY THE PICKER-UP, NOT THE LEAVER, which is why it persists: each of (2) and (3) took a full trace — read the implementation, then trace it to the consumer that reaches it — to establish as already-done. A brief item is not disproved by grep, because an existing function is not a wired one. ⚠️ SAME SHAPE ONE LAYER OVER, and it is what makes this worth a rule rather than four fixes: two RESIDUE rows filed on 2026-09-12 described state that was already repaired when they were filed — `2026-09-12-nsrr-spo2-holds-across-dropouts` by the very commit that filed it, and `2026-09-12-drawn-axis-consumers-not-migrated` by #2212 a week earlier. Same asymmetry: the artifact is written by a session that is leaving. REMEDY IS THE OWNER'S — it is a `CLAUDE.md` §📌 sentence binding the executing session, and this row exists so the evidence survives the sessions that gathered it. Do NOT route around it with a sixth status or a new convention | Verified 2026-09-13 against `origin/main` at `3f16585a`: each item traced to its implementation AND to the consumer that reaches it — `capture.py:6275` unions the blocked set into `protect` before `diskguard.prune_old_nights`; `trio-batch.mjs:1530` computes `disagrees` and `:1416` surfaces it; `git log -S` dates B4's wiring to 2026-09-03. The four brief instances are all in the two briefs this sweep was claimed to cover, so the rate is a property of the sample and is NOT a corpus-wide estimate | OPEN |
| 2026-09-13-scorer-snapshots-while-cache-is-incremental | 2026-09-13 | `tools/nsrr-oxydex-odi.mjs` | **Two tools read the same growing corpus and only ONE of them can be re-run cheaply, which is an inconsistency in the same session's work rather than a design decision.** `nsrr-criterion-sweep.mjs --cache` is incremental: it skips records already cached (keyed on id AND source size), detects still-arriving EDFs from their own header, and flushes atomically — so it can be re-run against a corpus that is still downloading and costs nothing for what it has already done. `nsrr-oxydex-odi.mjs` does none of that: `discover()` enumerates the directory ONCE at startup, so a run launched at 2271 records scores exactly those 2271 and never sees later arrivals, and a re-run rescores every record from scratch. Measured 2026-09-13: a run launched at 2271 was still executing when the corpus reached 4084, i.e. 1813 landed records it structurally could not include, and picking them up means paying for all 2271 again. ⚠️ The scorer also re-reads each 36 MB EDF from the NAS, so a re-run is hours of network I/O that competes with the download it is waiting on — the cost is not just CPU. Remedy is the one already built next door: cache per-record scores keyed on id and source size, so a re-run scores only what it has not scored. Not done here because the corpus is ~1 h from complete and one clean full run is cheaper than retrofitting now — that reasoning expires the moment a second growing corpus appears. | `grep -n 'discover(argv)' tools/nsrr-oxydex-odi.mjs` shows the single startup enumeration; `tools/nsrr-criterion-sweep.mjs` `buildCache` shows the incremental form with `srcBytes` and `edfExpectedBytes` for contrast | fixed #2508 |
| 2026-09-13-verity-emits-absent-stamps-at-scale | 2026-09-13 | `capture-host/capture.py` | **The Polar Verity Sense delivers PMD frames carrying a device stamp of exactly `0` — an ABSENT measurement — at a rate of thousands per day, and nothing has established what the device means by it.** The `_AbsentDeviceStamp` guard (#2405, 2026-09-12) refuses to derive a clock from such a frame, which is correct and is not what this row is about. The row is the UNDERLYING behaviour the guard exposed the moment it shipped: measured on vigil's journal, **7283 refusals in the first day of the guard being live** — 2694 on 2026-09-12 from 18:21:45 (the first line ever emitted) and 4589 on 2026-09-13 — and **100 % of them the Verity**, zero from the H10 and zero from the ring. `nightqc.arrival_quality`'s docstring independently records the same shape from the other side: every Verity `ppi` stream carries `last_sensor_ns` literally 0 for all 4864 packets, and 22 of 23 arrival refusals over 470 streams / 5 nights are this rather than skew. So two unrelated instruments, one live and one offline, agree that a whole stream's timing is absent rather than merely noisy. ⚠️ WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and must not be guessed: whether the zero is the device's deliberate sentinel for 'this PPI interval carries no timestamp', an unpopulated field in the frame layout we parse, or something our own parse drops — the §∅ CUT question 'the device emitted it' vs 'our path manufactured it', which for the O2Ring took a read of the vendor SDK's return path to settle and has not been done for this stream. Nor is it established whether any consumer spends the affected PPI intervals: if PPI is exported with a fabricated or interpolated time, that is the §∅ defect one layer down and is worse than the refusal. The guard makes the absence visible; it does not make the stream usable, and a stream whose timing is absent for every packet may not belong in an export at all | Measured 2026-09-13 against vigil's journal (`journalctl -u tepna-capture --since '14 days ago'`, 503 295 lines): 7283 `device stamp ABSENT (zero)` lines, all matching `Polar Sense 0C301E3F`, first at `Sep 12 18:21:45`; per-day 2694 / 4589. The guard's introducing commit is `b6ee520e` (2026-09-12), so the 14-day window contains only ~1 day of it. Corroborating offline count at `nightqc.py:1148` | fixed #2772 |

| 2026-09-14-union-merge-duplicates-a-closed-row | 2026-09-14 | `#2503` | **`briefs/RESIDUE.md merge=union` silently DUPLICATES any row that is CLOSED while another commit appends, because the ledger is not append-only and its own contract says so.** The attribute's justifying comment at `.gitattributes:43` reads *append-only at ONE end by rule (rows are added and closed, never edited)* — but CLOSING a row IS an edit: the contract is explicit that *a row is closed by changing only its state cell*. A union driver cannot represent an edit. It keeps BOTH sides' lines, so a rebase of a close against a concurrent append yields the SAME key twice — once OPEN, once fixed-#NNNN — and the two rows then contradict each other about whether the defect is live. The union choice is right for the APPEND half and wrong for the CLOSE half, and nothing in the comment distinguishes them. ⚠️ **IT IS A SILENT CORRUPTION AT MERGE TIME — corrected 2026-09-15 after reproducing it on live PRs.** The row first described this as producing a conflict you resolve. It does not. `git merge --no-commit` SUCCEEDS, `git merge-tree` emits ZERO conflict markers, and the duplicate appears anyway — measured on #2506 against `origin/main`: exit 0, 0 markers, 160 rows against 159 unique keys. So the hazard is not a merge you must resolve, it is a merge that looks like it worked. ⚠️ AND GITHUB DISAGREES WITH LOCAL GIT, IN THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION: the PR reads `DIRTY` on the platform while the local merge is clean, so the platform's refusal is the SAFE reading and anyone who resolves a DIRTY PR by merging locally and pushing ships the duplicate. Cause of the disagreement NOT established — GitHub's mergeability computation is not inspectable from here and "it does not apply .gitattributes merge=union" is an untested guess. What IS established: two gates catch it — `docs-ledger` check8c, and `residue-ids` since #2507 (verified on this exact case: `✗ appears MORE THAN ONCE`, exit 1) — so it cannot reach `main` unnoticed — but it must be hand-repaired on every close that races an append, and `tools/residue-ids.mjs` does NOT see it (it reported *none colliding, none mutated* on the duplicated tree). ⚠️ REMEDY IS A TRADEOFF, NOT A ONE-LINER, which is why this is filed rather than fixed: dropping `merge=union` restores the per-PR conflicts it was added to kill one day earlier (2026-09-13), so the options are union plus hand-repair on close, a custom driver that appends but refuses to duplicate a key, or closing rows only in a dedicated PR that cannot race. ⚠️ **A FOURTH MITIGATION EXISTS AND NEEDS NO DECISION — REBUILD THE EDIT ON CURRENT `main` INSTEAD OF MERGING OR REBASING ONTO IT** (Heron, 2026-09-15). A rebase is not a way around this: it REPLAYS the diff through the same union driver, which is how this very PR's rebase produced a duplicate of somebody else's row. Re-making the one-cell edit on a fresh branch off `main` sends the file through no merge driver at all, so no second copy can be produced. Cheap for a state-cell close, and it is what a closer should do by default until the tradeoff above is decided. ⚠️ Assert the CURRENT state before re-applying: Heron's rebuild found the row already read `fixed #2498` (closed by #2503), so the close was redundant and merging it would have re-applied an `OPEN → fixed` edit to a line that no longer said `OPEN` — the redundancy and the duplicate were the same fact from two sides. Read the COMMITTED HEAD for that check, never the working tree. | Reproduced 2026-09-14 on #2503: a one-cell close of `2026-09-14-vigil-sh-tests-leak-stub-servers`, rebased by `rebase-safe` onto a main carrying four new rows, produced 2 occurrences of that key (one OPEN, one fixed-#2498) and turned the commit's own diffstat from a modify into `1 file changed, 1 insertion(+)`. `docs-ledger` → `✕ check8c · row ids are unique (never reused) — 2026-09-14-vigil-sh-tests-leak-stub-servers`; `residue-ids --base origin/main` → `ok — 0 row(s) added, none colliding, none mutated`. Attribute at `.gitattributes:57`, its premise at `:43`. | OPEN |
| 2026-09-15-aai-normalisation-saturates | 2026-09-15 | `#2523` | **AAI's `/5` normalisation is at its ceiling on 45.3 % of SHHS1 records, so in NSI &#x2014; where it carries a full quarter of the score &#x2014; the term is a constant 1.0 for nearly half the cohort and discriminates nothing there.** Two consumers normalise the same way: `oxydex-dsp.js:2098` `aaiLoad = autoArousalIdx / 5` (SSI, weight 0.2) and `:4654` `Math.min(autoArousalIdx / 5, 1)` (NSI, one of four equally-weighted terms). The related UARS `+1: autonomic arousal index >= 3/hr` point (`OxyDex Reference.html`, UARS scoring block) fires on 66.7 % of the same records. A point that fires on two thirds of a population, and a normaliser saturated on half of it, are weaker discriminators than their construction implies. &#x26A0; **NOT A LICENCE TO RETUNE THE CONSTANTS, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE REASON THIS IS FILED RATHER THAN FIXED.** SHHS1 is attended PSG on an older cohort; OxyDex's actual users are home O2Ring wearers. The instrument is shared, the population is not, so recalibrating `/5` onto SHHS1 would tune the app to a cohort it does not serve &#x2014; the error of convicting deliberate behaviour with the wrong denominator. What is owed first is the same saturation fraction measured on the HOME corpus (`uploads/`, which has no expert arousal reference but does have AAI); only if it saturates there too is a constant in question. | 2026-09-15-proxy-resprate-uninformative | 2026-09-15 | `#2136` | **OxyDex's proxy respiration rate does not measure respiration rate: against two independent inductance belts it correlates at Pearson r = 0.046 (Spearman 0.082) and under-reads by 5.3 brpm, so the number in the export carries essentially no information about the subject's breathing.** `2026-09-03-oxydex-proxy-resprate-unattributed` recorded that the value ships with a `basis` and no estimator attribution, and its sibling `2026-09-03-oxydex-resprate-has-no-consumer` found nothing reads it &#x2014; both about PROVENANCE and REACH. Neither asked whether it is RIGHT. It is not. Median proxy 9.10 brpm against a belt reference of 14.34; only 3.5 % of nights land within 1 brpm of truth, and removing the median offset lifts that only to 33.8 %, so this is not a calibration error that a constant would fix &#x2014; an estimator with r = 0.05 has nothing to calibrate. The proxy's output is also COMPRESSED: 7.8-11.6 brpm against a true range of 8.5-25.2. &#x26A0; **A SECOND, PURELY STATIC DEFECT IN THE SAME KERNEL: the `Fast (>20)` label is UNREACHABLE BY CONSTRUCTION.** `_respRateProxyWindow` scans 20 bins over 0.13-0.33 Hz, so the largest value it can emit is 0.33 x 60 = 19.8 brpm, while `respRateLabel` emits `Fast (>20)` only above 20. One of the three labels can never be produced. Confirmed against the real exported kernel, not a copy of its arithmetic: driven with a pure 30 brpm tone it returns 19.2 and labels it `Normal (10-20)`. &#x26A0; NOT AN SHHS ARTIFACT &#x2014; the home corpus shows the same signature independently: 117 trio nights give proxy median 10.9 brpm, range 8.4-13.8, the same narrow low band, against a sleeping-adult expectation of 12-16. Two corpora, two devices, one compressed distribution. &#x26A0; MECHANISM IS A HYPOTHESIS, NOT A FINDING, and is stated so it can be tested rather than assumed: the kernel removes the MEAN but does not DETREND, and spectral power in heart rate falls with frequency, so a raw peak-power search over 0.13-0.33 Hz should be pulled toward its lowest bins by 1/f drift. The bottom-heavy output (median 9.10 against a band spanning 7.8-19.8) is consistent with that and does not establish it. &#x26A0; NO BEHAVIOUR CHANGED HERE. Whether an uninformative number should be nulled (it is a published value that carries no information &#x2014; the shape §&#x2205; names), detrended, or given a wider band is an owner decision, and each option moves `computeHash` and owes fixture re-verification. | `node tools/nsrr-score-pool.mjs --scorer ./nsrr-resprate-validate.mjs --limit 300`, 2026-09-15. 300 scored, 201 control-clean. CONTROL: the two belts agree exactly on 46/300 and within 0.63 brpm on 201/300, median abs-diff 0.360 brpm; the 99 records where they disagree are EXCLUDED, because there the reference is not trustworthy. True rate above the proxy's band on 8/201 = 4.0 %. Kernel at `oxydex-dsp.js:4989` (`_respRateProxyWindow`), band `0.13 + k*(0.33-0.13)/19` for k=0..19; label at `:5025`. Producer `oxydex-dsp.js:2871`, exported at `:7992`. | fixed #2527 |

| 2026-09-15-sidecar-floor-is-above-the-phenomenon | 2026-09-15 | `capture-host/writers.py` | **The run sidecar cannot record the thing it was built for: `RUN_MIN_BY_STREAM` maps every stream to `T_STUCK = 200` samples (~1.6 s at ~126 Hz), and CLAUDE.md §∅'s own measurement of the phenomenon is 149 runs, 105 of them >= 10 samples, the LONGEST 78 (0.62 s) — entirely below the floor.** This is a CATEGORY difference, not drift: `T_STUCK` asks "is this stream STUCK?" while the sidecar is read to answer "was there a blanking EVENT here?", which the in-JS `PIN_MIN_RUN = 5` asks. ⚠️ The consequence is a fabricated negative, not a missing feature: an empty sidecar reads as "clean night" when it means "nothing cleared a 1.6 s floor" — and `ppgdex-dsp.js:374`'s otherwise-correct rule that "THE FILE WINS for what was observed" would, if a reader were built on it today, UNDO the exclusion landed in #2531. Neither number was growth. This is the third framing of the same data and the first one built on the curve rather than on endpoints: a rate, then an endpoint, both single numbers standing in for a shape. &#x26A0; **OPERATIONAL CONSEQUENCE, which is the actionable part:** a `MemoryMax` sized from steady-state RSS (~250&#x2013;300 MB) would sit at roughly a THIRD of the startup transient and would OOM the daemon on every start. Cycle 1 ran 9.1 h (a quiet morning); cycles 2 and 3 ran ~1.9 h and ~0.5 h during an afternoon of landing PRs. **A 72 h unattended run therefore requires a deploy freeze, not merely a free box** &#x2014; which is a constraint on §17 of `OPERATIONAL-MATURITY-ROADMAP-2026-08-27-BRIEF.md`, whose boxes 16 and 17 remain the charter's gate. | OPEN |

| 2026-09-16-run-classifier-is-inverted | 2026-09-16 | `capture-host/writers.py` | **The zero-order-hold classifier keys on run-length CONCENTRATION, which a NEVER-REPEATING stream maximises — so it labels the most variable signal `held` and the documented zero-order hold `variable`.** `_decide` (`writers.py:790`) picks the adjacent run-length pair `(ln, ln+1)` with the highest combined share and sets `held` when that share &#x2265; `HELD_TOP2_SHARE` (0.95). A stream in which no sample ever repeats has EVERY run of length 1, so its length histogram is perfectly concentrated, `share = 1.000`, and it classifies `held`. Measured on the 2026-09-15 tri-device night (6.5 h, all three devices): **Verity PPG channels 0/1/2 &#x2014; `mean_run=1.00`, `top2=1,2`, `share=1.000`, `class=held` over 1,286,760 samples per channel**; O2Ring `accraw` X/Y &#x2014; **`mean_run=7.03`, `class=variable`**. &#x26A0; **`writers.py`'s own `RUN_MIN_BY_STREAM` comment says `accraw` is *"a zero-order hold; the classifier catches it"* &#x2014; and it does not.** Same shape as the H10 contact-bit comment closed by #2549: a comment asserting a behaviour the data contradicts, one layer from code. &#x26A0; **TODAY THE HARM IS BOUNDED, AND THAT IS EXACTLY WHY IT IS URGENT.** The class only suppresses the WARM-UP buffer: runs &#x2265; `min_run` (200) are emitted unconditionally by the branch above it, whose comment records that every non-sentinel run &#x2265; 200 was the failure mode. So at the shipped floor of 200 a wrong label costs almost nothing. **It stops being free the moment the floor drops** &#x2014; and a rail-keyed rule at floor 5 is OWNER-AUTHORIZED (D2, 2026-09-15) and not yet built. Under that rule a `held` label suppresses short rail runs, so the Verity PPG &#x2014; mislabelled `held` &#x2014; would have its blanking spans DROPPED, which is the §&#x2205; defect the rail rule exists to fix, reintroduced by the classifier. The inversion must be fixed BEFORE or WITH the floor change, not after. &#x26A0; **NOT ESTABLISHED:** whether `held` is reachable for a genuine hold at all on this corpus (only two classes were observed here, and the one true hold got `variable`); and whether the criterion was ever correct for some stream the corpus does not contain. Fixing it needs the criterion restated in terms of the property it is for &#x2014; a hold has LONG runs, so `mean_run` or a length threshold, not histogram concentration. | `/srv/tepna/captures/2026-09-15/`, sidecar headers written by the live daemon: `Polar_VeritySense_0C301E3F_20260915220844_PPGRUNS.txt` (`class=held ratio=1.0 top2=1,2 share=1.000 decided_at=64runs`, three channels, `# final … mean_run=1.00`) and `Wellue_O2Ring-S_S8AW2100_20260915221135_ACCRAWRUNS.txt` (`mean_run=7.03 class=variable`). Classifier at `writers.py:790-801`; consumer at `:806`; the contradicted comment in `RUN_MIN_BY_STREAM`. | fixed #2553 |
| 2026-09-16-devcaps-has-no-branch-consumer | 2026-09-16 | `BLE-TRANSPORT-REDESIGN-2026-09-10-BRIEF.md` | **§1.3's per-unit capability RECORD exists and is written, but NO CAPABILITY BRANCH READS IT — done-when 1 is open, and this row exists so the mechanism landing is not mistaken for the item being met.** `capture-host/devcaps.py` has a real producer (the `hr-flags-bit2` measurement in `run_polar` records both arms against the unit's address), persists across restarts, and reaches `status.json` as `devcaps`. What is missing is a decision that consults it. &#x26A0; **THIS IS DELIBERATE, NOT OVERSIGHT, AND THE REASON IS THE INTERESTING PART:** the only true per-unit capability in the daemon today, `_has_contact_bit`, is a **SESSION fact** — "have we seen the contact bit *this session*" — and NOT a capability. Seeding it from the record would coerce an unmeasured `None` into `False`, which is exactly the defect `devcaps.get` refuses and exactly what §1.3 exists to end. A branch can only read the record where absence can be **PROBED ON DEMAND**, which is done-when 2. &#x26A0; **The natural first conversion is `is_polar`-gated clock sync** (`capture.py:2677/2694/2706`), which infers a protocol capability from a CONFIG VENDOR STRING (`vendor == "polar"`) — a model-level assertion of exactly the kind §1.3 names. It cannot be converted yet because no clock-sync capability probe exists; writing that probe is the unit that unblocks done-when 1. &#x26A0; **`needs_pmd` and `optional` are NOT candidates** and should not be swept in: `needs_pmd` derives from the configured stream set and `optional` from operator intent. Both are CONFIGURATION, not hardware properties, and forcing them through a capability record would be the wrong shape — a capability record answers "what can this unit do", never "what did the operator ask for". | `capture-host/devcaps.py` (100 % covered, 15 tests; mutation-verified — a `False` default kills 6, an unnormalised address kills 1). Producer at `capture.py` `run_polar` beside `_has_contact_bit`; reader `STATUS["devcaps"]`; allowlisted in `tools/find_unwired.py` with this reason rather than silently passing. Branch inventory measured 2026-09-16: `is_polar` (3 sites, vendor string), `needs_pmd` (2 sites, config), `optional` (3 sites, config), `_has_contact_bit` (1 site, already per-unit and already correct). | fixed #2650 |

| 2026-09-16-maxstepms-names-two-populations | 2026-09-16 | `tools/trio-batch.mjs` | **`maxStepMs` is reported by two instruments over DIFFERENT ANCHOR SETS and they disagree by 57&#xD7; for the same device on the same night, with no field distinguishing them.** 2026-09-15 Polar H10: `arrival_*.json` reports **1796.7 ms** over **172,532** anchors; the same night's ECGDex export reports `recording.hostAxis.maxStepMs` = **31.5 ms** over **6,050**. Both come from the SAME `clock.js hostAxis` function &#x2014; the definition is identical and only the input differs, so neither is wrong and the pair is unusable for comparison across tools. &#x26A0; **The direction is the diagnostic and is counter-intuitive**: for a smooth drift, SPARSER anchors give LARGER steps, so denser-anchors-larger means the large value is a genuine outlier at packet resolution (one stalled BLE delivery between consecutive anchors) that a ~4 s view never samples. That reading makes 1796.7 ms **delivery jitter, not a device clock step** &#x2014; but it is an inference from the anchor geometry, not a measurement of the stall, and it was NOT confirmed against the link log. &#x26A0; **IT ALSO EXCEEDS A DOCUMENTED BOUND.** Clock Contract §7 records BLE delivery jitter as *"~0.1 s, up to 470 ms observed"*. 1796.7 ms is 3.8&#xD7; that maximum, so either the bound is stale or this is not jitter. Both readings matter and neither is settled here. &#x26A0; §7 already requires `ppm` to be quoted with its span for exactly this reason; `maxStepMs` has no equivalent rule and needs one &#x2014; a step figure without its anchor count and spacing is not comparable to another. | `uploads/trio/2026-09-15/arrival_2026-09-15.json` (`anchors 172532`, `maxStepMs 1796.7`, `spanSec 23268`) vs `ECGDex_2026-09-15.node-export.json` (`hostAxis.anchors 6050`, `maxStepMs 31.48`, `spanMs 23268586`). Shared implementation at `clock.js` `hostAxis`; surfaced by `tools/trio-batch.mjs:1365`. | withdrawn 2026-09-19-maxstep-juxtaposed-at-two-densities |

| 2026-09-16-patsd-zero-is-a-fallback | 2026-09-16 | `qrs-equiv-analysis.js` | **`patJitterSdMs` publishes `0` when the quantity was NOT MEASURED, and `0` when it genuinely measured no excess jitter &#x2014; two different claims wearing one number (§&#x2205;).** `qrs-equiv-analysis.js:302` reads `patSD = baEP &amp;&amp; baPP &amp;&amp; baPP.sd &gt; baEP.sd ? Math.sqrt(baPP.sd*baPP.sd - baEP.sd*baEP.sd) : 0`. The `: 0` branch is taken in THREE cases: the ECG&minus;Pulse Bland-Altman is absent, the PPG&minus;Pulse one is absent, or the optical SD does not exceed the electrical floor. Only the third is a measurement of zero; the first two are absences, and the published value is identical. The quantity is not incidental &#x2014; `papers/rmssd-equivalence.html` headlines it as *'the quadrature excess over the electrical floor &#x2014; was ~4.0 ms, i.e. essentially all of the PPG disagreement is pulse-arrival-time jitter'*, so a fabricated 0 reads as *the optical arm carries no PAT jitter*, which is that paper's central claim negated. &#x26A0; OBSERVED, not hypothesised: a run at `nSubj: 240` returned `patJitterSdMs: 0` together with only ONE of the three pairs (`ppg_minus_ecg`), because `baEP` and `baPP` both require the `pulse` arm while `baPE` does not. The 0 was therefore reported by a run in which the quantity could not be computed at all, and nothing in the result object says so. &#x26A0; SIBLING DEFECT IN THE SAME OBJECT: `RESULT.pairs[...]` is populated `if (p[1])`, so a missing comparison is ABSENT rather than present-and-null. A consumer cannot distinguish *this pair was not computed* from *this tool never emits that pair*, which is how a partial run reads as a complete one &#x2014; it is what made a re-cut of this paper look ready while two of its three headline comparisons were missing. &#x26A0; WHY the `pulse` arm was empty is NOT established here and is not guessed at. That is the next unit, and it blocks re-cutting `rmssd-equivalence` under cohort-gen 2.0. | Site `qrs-equiv-analysis.js:302` (`patSD`); pairs map at `:371-377`. Run: `node tools/analysis-rerun.mjs --paper-scale --only qrs-equiv-analysis.html`, 2026-09-16 &#x2192; `nWindows 220`, `pairs` containing only `ppg_minus_ecg` (`n 220, biasMs 3.152, pearson 0.8152`), `patJitterSdMs 0`. Published values it should reproduce: ECG&minus;Pulse bias &minus;0.02 ms r 0.9999; optical bias ~+1 % r 0.92; PAT excess ~4.0 ms. | OPEN |

Whether each is affected is NOT established per-tool here; `cgm-hrv-coupling` reproduced its published values exactly, which admits two readings that this row does not choose between: it does not depend on the broken path, or its published numbers were themselves produced after 2026-07-16 and embed the same defect. Establishing which is the next unit and it gates any re-cut of those papers. &#x26A0; REMEDY NOT APPLIED HERE: the load path needs classicified sources (either give `build-analysis.mjs` ownership of `cohort-harness.html` so it inlines them, or strip on load). That is a shared-surface change and is owed a deliberate unit. A tripwire is owed with it &#x2014; the harness should REFUSE rather than serve nulls when a node fails to initialise. | Reproduction: load `qrs-equiv-analysis.html` headless, set `nSubj` to 6, click `#run`, capture `pageerror` &#x2192; `SyntaxError: Unexpected token 'export'`; `window.QRS_EQUIV.pairs` = `['ppg_minus_ecg']` only, `patJitterSdMs` = 0. Sites: `cohort-harness.html:41-52` (loader), `:49-53` (`SCRIPTS`); `pulsedex-dsp.js:1737`; `oxydex-dsp.js:8119`; `glucodex-dsp.js:2236`. It is wired (`nightqc.py:1906`, `:2497`) and RAN on the 2026-09-16 night, publishing `class_b`/`clip`/`clips` into `QC-SUMMARY.json` and catching an ECG clip of 141 samples at value 18364. &#x26A0; **THE TWO LAYERS ARE COMPLEMENTARY BY DESIGN, NOT REDUNDANT.** The live sidecar answers *"is this stream STUCK right now"* under a P0 constraint that forbids windows and medians on the notification path; the back-check answers *"where was the signal ABSENT"* with the whole recording in hand. A floor of 200 is correct for the first question. Reading the first layer's output as if it were the second is what produced the original row. &#x26A0; **A DECISION WAS TAKEN ON THE WRONG FRAMING.** `OWNER-DECISION-QUEUE` D2 (2026-09-15) authorized *"a new rail-keyed sidecar rule in `writers.py` (floor 5)"* on the strength of that row; the D2 note is corrected in the same change. Nothing was built against it, so the cost was the authorization itself, not code. &#x26A0; **HOW IT WAS CAUGHT, because the method is the transferable part:** the claim "never built" came from grepping `writers.py` alone. Checking the BRIEF that owns the rule, and then the layer it names, took one query each. This is the fourth would-be rebuild stopped this session by looking at the tree before building (`pin-coverage`, `doc-search` prior art, §2's fake state machine) and the first where the wrong claim had already been reported to the owner. | `PPG-ABSENCE-AS-VALUE-2026-09-06-BRIEF.md` §3; `capture-host/nightqc.py:1555` (`_CLIP_MIN_RUN`), `:1703-1717` (rail constants + `rail_value`), `:1762` (`_at_rail`), `:1778` (`_rail_runs`), `:1822` (`clip_regions`), `:1866` (`class_b_runs`); call sites `:1906`, `:2497`. | 2026-09-17-mutation-hygiene-misses-a-split-read | 2026-09-17 | `capture-host/tests/test_mutation_hygiene.py` | **`test_no_test_reads_a_mutatable_module_source_raw` is a PER-LINE check, so a raw read split across two lines passes it — and one already had.** The gate requires every source scan of a mutatable module to route through `tests/_srcscan.module_source`, because a raw read makes mutmut report the module as *"failed to collect stats"*: it looks like a broken environment and the module is **silently never measured**. &#x26A0; **A live example shipped before this was noticed.** `tests/test_offline_op_rate.py` (#2589, merged) read `capture.py` with `open(os.path.join(...,` on one line and `"capture.py")).read()` on the next; the per-line scan saw a `READ_CALL` with no module name on one line and a module name with no read call on the other, and reported nothing. The identical read written on ONE line in `tests/test_link_error_rate.py` was caught immediately, which is what surfaced the asymmetry. Both are now routed; the gate is not. &#x26A0; **The per-line design is itself a deliberate fix, and this is not an argument to revert it** &#x2014; its own comment records that `if SANCTIONED in src` (per-FILE) let one routed read exempt every other read in the same file, and that the largest file in the suite held a blanket exemption earned by its own import line. The remedy is to keep per-line matching and add continuation handling (join logical lines, or match the statement via `ast`), not to widen the scope back. &#x26A0; **Neither the count nor the population is established here.** One offender was found by coincidence, not by a sweep; how many other split reads exist across the suite is UNMEASURED, and a fix should begin by counting them. | Gate at `capture-host/tests/test_mutation_hygiene.py`; sanctioned helper `tests/_srcscan.module_source`. Offender was `tests/test_offline_op_rate.py` as merged in #2589 (now routed); the single-line equivalent in `tests/test_link_error_rate.py` failed the gate on its first run, 2026-09-17. | fixed #2776 |
| 2026-09-17-mutation-scope-selects-whole-functions | 2026-09-17 | `capture-host/tools/mutate_diff.py` | **The diff-scoped mutation gate selects whole FUNCTIONS, so a one-line edit inside a device runner selects the entire runner — measured 41&#xD7; amplification, and the job is then SIGKILLed with no verdict.** On #2590: 59 changed lines selected **2422 lines across 5 functions** (`run_polar` 1038 · `run_oxyii` 1119 · `run_viatom` 147 · `polar_offline_op` 92 · `_log_link_error` 26). The change to each runner was ONE line — replacing `log.warning(...)` with `_log_link_error(...)` — and each pulled its whole runner in. &#x26A0; **IT PRESENTS AS A MUTATION FAILURE AND IS NOT ONE.** `mutation (diff-scoped)` reported `failure`, which reads as "this PR has surviving mutants"; the log shows `Generating mutants` spinning from 15:59:26 to 17:37:44 and then **exit 143 — SIGTERM**. 98 minutes, killed, no mutant ever scored. CLAUDE.md §4c names this shape exactly: a gate that dies with no verdict is not your diff. &#x26A0; **REPRODUCIBLE OFF CI, which is what rules out a runner problem:** `python3 tools/mutate_diff.py --base origin/main~2 --report-only` hangs locally too and had to be killed. The SELECTION completes and prints its five functions; what does not complete is mutant generation over 2422 lines. &#x26A0; **TWO PRs hit it consecutively** (#2589 `polar_offline_op`, #2590 the three runners) while a docs-only branch and a peer's branch passed the same job the same day &#x2014; so the trigger is "touched a large function in `capture.py`", not "touched `capture.py`". Anyone editing a device runner will hit it. &#x26A0; **FUNCTION GRANULARITY IS DEFENSIBLE AND SHOULD NOT SIMPLY BE NARROWED TO LINES.** A changed line's behaviour genuinely depends on its enclosing function, and line-scoped mutation would miss a mutant that only misbehaves via state set earlier in the same function. What is NOT defensible is that the gate has no bound: the honest options are a per-run budget that REFUSES and says so (a refusal is a verdict; a SIGTERM is not), or splitting the 1000-line runners, which is a much larger change. &#x26A0; **NOT MEASURED:** how many functions in `capture-host/` exceed the size where this bites, and therefore how much of the module is effectively un-mutatable. One PR is not a survey. | CI job `mutation (diff-scoped)` on #2590, run 35241774526 job 105271877380: `Generating mutants` 15:59:26 &#x2192; 17:37:44, `exit code 143`. Local repro `python3 capture-host/tools/mutate_diff.py --base origin/main~2 --report-only`, killed at 250 s after printing `capture.py: 59 changed line(s) in 5 function(s)`. Function sizes measured from `capture.py` by def-to-next-def span. | fixed #2776 |
| 2026-09-17-mutation-un-mutatable-surface-measured | 2026-09-17 | `capture-host/tools/mutate_diff.py` | **Fills the "NOT MEASURED" caveat in `2026-09-17-mutation-scope-selects-whole-functions`: the un-mutatable surface is THREE functions, not a module.** Measured by `ast` over `capture-host/*.py` — 1576 functions (methods included), 36,825 function lines: **&#x2265;800 lines: 3 functions, 3755 lines = 10.2 %** · &#x2265;400: 4 fn, 11.5 % · &#x2265;200: 12 fn, 17.7 %. The three are `webmon.py:make_app` (1602), `capture.py:run_oxyii` (1117), `capture.py:run_polar` (1036); the next largest is `pull_session.py:_pull_once` at 473, so there is a **2.2&#xD7; gap** between the third and the fourth. &#x2705; **THAT CHANGES THE SHAPE OF ANY FIX.** The failing selection was 2422 lines, and only these three can contribute a four-figure span on their own, so "the mutation gate cannot run on `capture.py`" is wrong — it cannot run on changes that touch `run_oxyii`, `run_polar`, or `make_app`. 1564 of 1576 functions are under 200 lines. A budget-with-refusal would therefore refuse rarely and specifically, rather than becoming a routine outcome. &#x26A0; **MY FIRST MEASUREMENT OF THIS WAS WRONG AND ITS OWN OUTPUT SAID SO.** Spanning def-to-next-module-level-def reported `writers.py:_phone_ts` — a timestamp formatter — at **1081 lines**, because the regex matched only unindented `def` and any function followed by a class absorbed that class's whole body. It inflated the &#x2265;200 share to 23.5 % and invented two entries in the top eight. `ast` end_lineno is exact and `_phone_ts` disappears from the list entirely. A function-size measurement that cannot see class methods is measuring text layout, not functions. &#x26A0; **NOT a recommendation to split them.** `run_polar`/`run_oxyii` are device runners whose length is structural, and splitting them to satisfy a mutation budget would be the tail wagging the dog; the bound belongs in the gate. This row sizes the problem and does not prescribe. | `python3` + `ast` over `capture-host/*.py`, 2026-09-17, counting `FunctionDef`/`AsyncFunctionDef` spans as `end_lineno - lineno + 1`. Compare the superseded regex method, which reported 1109 functions / 40,365 lines and `_phone_ts` at 1081. | fixed #2776 |
