| 2026-09-18-robust-caching-is-not-a-reachable-ceiling | 2026-09-18 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **WITHDRAWS the significance claim of `2026-09-18-as11-implements-robust-caching`, whose OBSERVATION is correct and whose CEILING reading is not. That row reports the AS11 exposing `0x2B29` CLIENT SUPPORTED FEATURES beside `0x2B2A` DATABASE HASH and concludes the merged oracle "only POLLS the weaker half" of a protocol whose push half was available — "a ceiling difference, not a bug". The observation holds and is preserved. The ceiling does not exist, for three independent measured reasons.** (1) **NOT REACHABLE FROM THIS STACK** — the bound that row flagged UNKNOWN and deliberately left open. In pinned bleak, `0x2B29` occurs ONLY as a label in `uuids.py`'s name table (`0x2B29: "Client Supported Features"`); the BlueZ backend never writes it, and bleak's sole cache concept is `dangerous_use_bleak_cache`, its own service cache, unrelated to GATT caching. So no application-layer client here can set the Robust Caching bit, and "the unit was scoped believing polling was the best available" attributes a stack constraint to a design choice. (2) **ROBUST CACHING ANSWERS A DIFFERENT QUESTION THAN THE ORACLE ASKS — the strongest reason.** The parent's measured defect (#2372, `GATT-HANDLE-MAP` §1) is that bleak snapshots the D-Bus object tree WHILE IT IS STILL BEING POPULATED: seven events, six byte-identical, holding `00001801-…[]` alone with zero characteristics. Service Changed / Database Out Of Sync fire when the server's database CHANGES. Nothing changed — our VIEW was incomplete on that connect. So the bit, even settable and pushed perfectly, leaves the race untouched. The oracle is not a weaker substitute for push; it is an independent completeness check on a mirror we do not own. (3) **THE CAUSE OF THE UNBONDED FAILURE MAKES THE PUSH HALF MOOT BY CONSTRUCTION.** §1 measures BlueZ gating its GATT cache on `gatt_cache_is_enabled() = device_is_paired()`, and §2 records Database Hash as "the mechanism BlueZ itself uses on the bonded path that has never failed here". With the cache DISABLED there is no cached table to invalidate, so a change-notification protocol has nothing to act on — the remedy direction was already found and is bonding (`2026-09-10-cpap-unbonded-reason-was-wrong`), not a client feature bit. ⚠️ **The FACT is kept, not discarded** — it is already machine-readable in `gattmap.json` rather than only in prose, and it would matter again only to a raw-ATT client, which the parent's §3 refuses. NO CODE CHANGE: nothing was built, and ATT `0x12` remains handled nowhere on purpose, because a handler for a path no layer here can reach would be speculation, not coverage. | Read-only, 2026-09-18, this checkout at `origin/main` + the box. **bleak (pinned, in `capture-host/.venv`):** `grep -rn` over the package gives `uuids.py:617: 0x2B29: "Client Supported Features"` and `exc.py:240/305` `0x12 → DATABASE_OUT_OF_SYNC` — a NAME and an ERROR LABEL, no writer; `grep -iE '2b29\|supported_features\|write.*features' backends/bluezdbus/*.py` returns EMPTY. **Our tree:** `0x12`/"out of sync" appears in none of `capture.py`, `gattmap.py`, `as11_link.py`, `as11_pull.py`. **`/srv/tepna/captures/gattmap.json` (vigil, 2016 B, mtime Sep 18 16:35):** TWO units, which partially closes that row's own NOT-OBSERVED bound — `04:CD:15:3A:0B:BD` db_hash `4bcd397d247e`, 14 chars, carries BOTH `00002b29` and `00002b2a`; `24:AC:AC:0C:30:1E` (Polar OUI) **db_hash `None`, 21 chars, NEITHER characteristic** — exactly the heterogeneity `gattmap.py`'s module note already handles (`expected()` answers only for `None` on such a device). ⚠️ The Polar MODEL is not established by this evidence: identity is address-only (standing ruling), and `devcaps.json` holds a different Polar address (`24:AC:AC:02:84:96`), so H10-vs-Verity is unresolved and the remaining units stay NOT OBSERVED. ⚠️ Claim (3) is a consequence of the parent brief's OWN measurement, not a reading of BlueZ source, which I did not open | fixed #2649 |

| 2026-09-20-docs-papers-md-owned-by-nobody | 2026-09-20 | `tools/build-docs.mjs` | A served copy under `docs/papers/` is regenerated when it is HTML and never when it is Markdown, so editing a root `.md` leaves its served twin silently stale and `verify:docs` reports "docs/ current" with the stale file in the tree. The two look identical to a contributor and behave oppositely | Hit during #2699: `papers/PAPERS-AUDIT.md` was corrected, `docs/papers/PAPERS-AUDIT.md` kept the retracted figure through a full `build-docs.mjs` run, and `verify:docs` still reported current. `papers/papers.html` regenerated normally in the same run, which is what makes the gap easy to miss. Synced BY HAND there. The ownership is deliberate and documented at `tools/build-docs.mjs:74-83` (rebase-safe once treated all of `docs/` as generated and silently reverted an authored spec), so the fix is NOT to widen ownership blindly. MEASURED 2026-09-20, and the population is small enough to pin as an equality rather than a floor: of **39** `docs/**.md`, exactly **2** have a root twin and **BOTH DIFFER** — `docs/papers/PAPERS-AUDIT.md` is one line behind (a paper row reading Draft v3 against the root's v4) and `docs/papers/RERUN-RESULTS.md` is missing **162 lines**, an entire CONVERGED result section dated 2026-08-15, from the served copy. The other 37 are authored with no root original and are correctly owned by nobody. So the served site has been missing a month-old published result while `verify:docs` reported current, and the remedy is a two-file equality check plus a sync, NOT widening builder ownership over `docs/` | fixed #2701 |

| 2026-09-20-guard-denial-takes-the-whole-invocation | 2026-09-20 | `.claude/hooks/guard-shared-tree.sh` | **A `PreToolUse` guard denies the ENTIRE Bash invocation, so a correct objection to one clause silently cancels every unrelated clause beside it — and nothing reports which ran.** `guard-shared-tree.sh:34` reads `.tool_input.command` and matches its rules against the whole string (`:50` — *"the RAW command with line-continuations and newlines folded to spaces"*), so its verdict is necessarily all-or-nothing over the invocation. &#x26A0; **THE REPORTABLE PROPERTY IS THE SILENCE, NOT THE BREADTH.** A denial that announces itself costs one retry; this one costs a FALSE BELIEF ABOUT STATE — the operator is told a rule was violated, and is told nothing about the work that was cancelled to enforce it. Same shape as a push returning rc=0 while carrying nothing. A row read as "the guard is over-broad" sends the next reader to fix the wrong property: the rule that fired was RIGHT. &#x26A0; **NOT the same defect as a guard matching prose that merely DESCRIBES a forbidden form** (a heredoc quoting one, a commit message naming a glob). That is a parser problem and wants a parser fix; this is a blast-radius problem and wants either clause-level evaluation or a denial message that ENUMERATES what did not run. &#x26A0; **And the two remedies are not equal in cost, stated so nobody sizes this from the row alone:** clause-level evaluation means splitting a shell command into clauses, which is shell PARSING — quoting, nesting, substitutions — and a guard that parses shell wrongly fails OPEN, which is worse than the over-denial it replaces. The enumerating message is far cheaper and fixes the reported property directly: it does not reduce the blast radius, it removes the SILENCE, which is what made this cost a false belief rather than a retry. Two root causes, deliberately two rows. &#x26A0; **And the existing exemption does not reach it:** `:118-131` exempts the temp-index rescue recipe because that pattern is genuinely safe. Every exemption of that kind answers *"this command should not have been denied"*; here the command SHOULD have been denied, and the loss is the innocent clause | Measured 2026-09-20 (Heron), one instance, mine. A single Bash call chained `gh pr create --title ... --body-file /tmp/pr14.md` and then `git worktree remove ... --force`. The guard denied the invocation on the `--force` clause — correctly, per CLAUDE.md §👥.2 — and **the PR was never created**. Nothing said so: the denial text is entirely about `--force`. I learned it only because an unrelated follow-up command failed on the now-missing `/tmp/pr14.md`, which the same cancelled invocation had been going to write; had the body file already existed, the next signal would have been a peer asking where the PR was. &#x26A0; **n=2, and the second instance occurred WHILE COMMITTING THIS ROW.** The commit message for it DESCRIBED the forbidden flag in prose; the guard matched the description and denied the whole call, so the `git fetch`, `git add`, `git commit` and `git push` chained beside it all silently did not run — verified by `git status` immediately after: nothing staged, no new commit. **The defect demonstrated itself in the one command that documents it**, and the trigger was the OTHER root cause (prose describing a forbidden form), which is why these are two rows and not one. &#x26A0; **Bound: both instances mine, both caught within one command.** No rate and no realised cost is claimed — only the mechanism and the absent signal. Guard source read at `origin/main` | fixed #2871 |

The answer sat five lines below the line I cited | Measured 2026-09-20 on vigil: `busctl get-property org.bluez /org/bluez/hciN org.bluez.AdvertisementMonitorManager1 SupportedMonitorTypes` → "No such interface" on hci0–hci3; `systemctl cat bluetooth.service` ExecStart has no `--experimental`, no drop-in. | 2026-09-20-guard-strips-quotes-for-one-rule-only | 2026-09-20 | `.claude/hooks/guard-shared-tree.sh` | **The quote-and-heredoc stripping that makes a commit message safe to write is wired into exactly ONE of the hook's twelve rules, so whether a session may write down what it did depends on which rule the thing it did belongs to.** The `commit` rule (`:155`) matches `$cmd_noquotes`, which blanks quoted strings and heredoc bodies; the other eleven match `$cmdn` RAW. The header at `:39-44` documents this exact failure for that one rule, including its 2026-08-05 fix and the measured instance that forced it — a message that merely mentions a forbidden form, *"as one documenting THIS FILE must"*, was denied. **The reasoning generalises to every rule; the fix did not.** The consequence is nastier than an ordinary false positive because of WHICH artifact triggers it: the commit message is the artifact whose entire job is to describe what was done, so the more precisely a session documents a forbidden operation, the more reliably it is blocked from recording it. The available workaround is to describe the operation instead of quoting it, which degrades exactly the artifact that should be most precise. &#x26A0; **It cannot be fixed by widening `$cmd_noquotes` to all rules, and the header says why at `:57-59`:** the add/reset/clean rules match raw precisely so that a blanket add nested inside a `bash -c` string is still caught, and stripping quotes there would reopen it. The discriminator is POSITION, not quoting — a `-m`/`-F`/`--message` argument is never executed, whereas `bash -c`'s argument is — so the fix is to blank the message-flag argument for ALL rules and leave every other quoted string raw. &#x26A0; **THAT REMEDY HAS A COST, and it is the same class the remedy is meant to avoid: a mis-written blanking regex FAILS OPEN.** Measured 2026-09-20 against a sentinel clause — a greedy `-m[[:space:]]+".*"` spans TWO message clauses and blanks a chained command sitting between them, so a forbidden form nested there becomes invisible to every rule; the character-class form `-m[[:space:]]+"[^"]*"` holds on the same three cases, including an escaped quote inside the message. One character-class choice separates a working guard from a blind one. The existing `cmd_noquotes` already strips character-class-wise (`:38`), so the precedent is in the file — which makes the fix tractable, not free. Size it as a change that must be self-tested with a fail-open decoy, not as a one-line regex | Measured 2026-09-20 by feeding synthetic PreToolUse JSON to the hook: three commit messages identical but for the form they document. `git commit -a` → ALLOWED (rule `:155`, stripped). A forced worktree removal → DENIED (rule `:376`, raw). A force-clean → DENIED (rule `:350`, raw). Same artifact, same act, opposite outcomes, decided solely by which rule owns the documented form. &#x26A0; **First attempt at that measurement reproduced the defect on itself:** the three cases were written as one compound command, the third matched, and the whole call was denied — so the two cases containing nothing forbidden never ran and had to be re-issued singly. Instances: Heron's #2708 commit (denied on its message, landed by describing rather than quoting), this test harness, and two earlier of my own. &#x26A0; The per-call rather than per-clause blast radius is a SEPARATE root cause with its own row in #2708; these are two rows because the same event needs both descriptions and neither explains it alone | fixed #2871 |
| 2026-09-20-unpinned-radio-wedge-seen-only-at-failover | 2026-09-20 | `CAPTURE-HOST-RESOURCE-ORCHESTRATION-AUDIT-2026-09-05-BRIEF.md` | The HCI round trip that can prove a radio deaf is asked about the PINNED adapter every poll, and about a SPARE only at the instant a failover is being considered. Convicting files on a grep would be an invariant that convicts working code. | ⚠️ **TWO INDEPENDENT COUNTS DISAGREE, AND THAT IS THE POINT — do not quote either as the size.** Osprey 2026-09-20 over `capture-host/tests/*.py`: a kwarg-swallowing-lambda pattern matched **58 files**, a sleep-fake pattern matched **35**, top file `test_capture_runners.py` at 62 combined occurrences. | 2026-09-20-processnight-mutates-its-input | 2026-09-20 | `oxydex-dsp.js` | `OxyDex.processNight(rows)` EDITS THE CALLER'S ARRAY IN PLACE: `trimSensorWarmup` splices head and tail rows and `cleanArtifactHR` rewrites values, deliberately and documented at `oxydex-dsp.js:898` so every downstream reader sees the trimmed set. It is a trap for any TOOL that keeps a reference to `rows` and indexes by original position after the call: the array is shorter and shifted, and nothing says so. `tools/nsrr-oxstat-validate.mjs` did exactly that on 2026-09-15 -- ran `processNight` on the as-shipped rows, then masked those same rows against `OX stat[i]` -- so the status index was misaligned on every record the DSP trimmed (10 rows on shhs1-200981, 164 on shhs1-200233). Its paired design was not paired. Fixed in #2725 by giving each arm its own rows and taking every comparison count BEFORE a DSP call; the corrected figures reproduce the original to within one record, so the finding stood and only its instrument was wrong | Found by a self-consistency check added in #2725 (the adapter's own flagged count must equal an independent mask count; two routes to one number, disagreement = instrument fault) -- it failed on 2 of the first 6 records and passes 278/278 after the fix. Callers in tools/ at 2026-09-20, from `git grep -l processNight( -- tools/*.mjs`: `nsrr-aai-validate.mjs`, `nsrr-oxstat-validate.mjs` (fixed), `oxydex-export-staleness.mjs`, `pb-operating-point.mjs`. CANDIDATES, NOT CONVICTIONS: a caller that never re-indexes the array after the call is correct, and the three unchecked ones were not read. Remedy options, neither taken here: document the mutating contract at the entry point in words a caller reads first, or have the tool lane copy on entry. Changing `processNight` to copy is a DSP edit that moves OxyDex's `computeHash` for no output change | fixed #2757 |

The sweep's own §4 example — `2026-09-02-ppgdex-ambient-collected-unused` citing `ppgdex-dsp.js:679` for a store that had moved to `:934` — is the same class. Remedy, stated as a rule for new rows rather than a rewrite of 200 old ones: **cite by IDENTIFIER (function, constant, string) with the line and sha as a dated hint**, so a verifier greps the identifier and the line is a shortcut, never the claim. A tool that re-resolves each row's cited lines against current main and reports drift is the mechanical form; not built | Measured 2026-09-20: the three-hop chain in §1.2 re-resolved by identifier at `24edf8c2` — one of three line numbers had moved in two days (`:5306` → `:5520`), two held. | 2026-09-21-updater-skipped-the-serve-half-of-every-deploy | 2026-09-21 | `capture-host/tepna-update.sh` | **The hourly updater never served the bundles: steps 3 (`sync-apps.sh`) and 4 (`check-system-files.sh`) were guarded by `[ -x ]` while both scripts are committed 0644 and run through `bash`.** The pull happened, the daemon restarted, and `/srv/tepna/app` rotted — the exact rot `sync-apps.sh`'s own header was written to end (2026-07-26), re-created one layer up by the guard in the caller. The exec-bit gate (`test_a_unit_that_directly_execs_a_repo_script_requires_the_exec_bit`) covers systemd's execve, where 0644 FAILS loudly; for a bash-invoked script the same mode passes and an `-x` guard SKIPS silently, so /etc drift was never reported by the timer either. Fix: `-f` guards + two plant tests at mode 0644 (red on the old updater, green on the new). `PAT Feasibility.html` + `pat-feasibility.js` + `pat-gate.js` not served is a separate whitelist gap, left open here. | fixed #2748 |

The O2Ring CSV path never carried a null here (`parseCSV` drops invalid rows), so the shipped bundle's own numbers were right; the NSRR path did, because `to1Hz` correctly writes null for an out-of-range sample (#2423) and, since #2725, for every status-flagged sample too -- so the OX stat fix WIDENED the inflation by exactly the flagged fraction (median 5.4 % of a night) while leaving ODI-4 clean, because the ODI detector skips null rows and computeStats did not. What remains is that every SHHS T90 / mean SpO2 published before this fix is inflated by that night's dropout-plus-flagged fraction, and the surfaces are the same ones `2026-09-20-shhs-figures-predate-oxstat` names -- this row adds a SECOND reason those figures moved and a DIRECTION for T90 (down) | Mechanism read off `oxydex-dsp.js computeStats` and `nsrr-adapter.js:439` (`out.t90 = night.stats.t90pct`) on 2026-09-21; demonstrated in the new `primary-builder` gate group with a half-measured night: mean 46 -> 92, T95 100 % -> 100 % of MEASURED seconds, T90 100 % -> 0. | 2026-09-21-nightqc-budget-test-passed-over-zero-rows | 2026-09-21 | `capture-host/tests/test_nightqc.py` | **A test written to be the witness that the timing budget reaches the per-stream record had examined nothing since the day it was written.** `test_the_budget_reaches_the_per_stream_record` planted a `Polar_H10_…_ECG.csv`, called `nightqc.arrival_quality(night_dir)` — which lists only `*_PMDARRIVAL.csv` — and asserted `all("u_time" in r for r in rows)`: `rows` was `[]`, `all([])` is `True`, and the test has been green over an empty population for its whole life. | 2026-09-21-fiducial-jitter-scaled-every-file-by-the-last-fs | 2026-09-21 | `tools/pat-fiducial-jitter.mjs` | **A loop-carried variable made a published instrument silently wrong on any multi-rate batch.** `fsSeen` was assigned inside the per-file loop and read after it, so `pairwiseJitter(beats, fsSeen)` converted EVERY file's sample-index jitter to ms with the LAST file's `fs`. A batch mixing 55 Hz and 176 Hz Verity files (both exist in `/srv/data/tepna-corpus`, 18.14 vs 5.67 ms per sample) was scaled by one wrong quantum on the files not read last, and the DOMINANCE verdict (`MATERIAL` / `INTERMEDIATE` / `NOT-DOMINANT`, banded in ms) moved with directory order. This is the mechanism behind `2026-09-05-fiducial-sd-quoted-without-its-sample-rate`, which recorded the symptom (two runs, two verdicts) without the cause — the tool DID print an fs; it was the wrong one for most of its files. Fixed by per-file `fs` into `pairwiseJitter`, a report STRATIFIED by nominal rate with each SD's size in samples printed beside the ms, the TCH decomposition on the largest stratum only, and a warning when strata are mixed. `strataOf`/`stratum` pinned by 4 selftests. Every fiducial-family SD this tool published before 2026-09-21 on a mixed-rate batch is suspect; single-rate batches were unaffected | Measured 2026-09-21 on 3 × 176 Hz + 3 × 55 Hz Verity files: the same family reads 21.99 ms (1.21 samples) at 55 Hz and 3.38 ms (0.60 samples) at 176 Hz — `MATERIAL` vs `NOT-DOMINANT` — which under the old code would have been one number in whichever unit the last file carried. `git blame` puts `fsSeen` at the tool's birth; no run with a mixed batch is recorded in a brief with its file order, so the affected published figures cannot be enumerated from the repository | fixed #2749 |
| 2026-09-21-two-fast-forwarders-one-serve-step | 2026-09-21 | `capture-host/tepna-update.sh` | **Two units fast-forward `/opt/tepna` — `tepna-update.timer` (hourly, drifting) and `tepna-sync-main.timer` (`tools/sync-main.sh`, hourly at ~:01) — and the serve step lived inside the updater's "this run moved the ref" branch, so whenever sync-main won the hour the served tree stayed stale under an already-fixed guard.** #2748 fixed the `-x` guard; the first tick after it (10:02:34) read "up to date at abbbf15d — nothing to do" because sync-main had moved 7220e686 → abbbf15d at 10:01:18, and `sync-apps --check` still read 30 of 35 stale. A deploy half keyed on an EVENT one process observes is skipped by every other process that produces the same state. Step 3 is now keyed on the served tree's STATE (`sync-apps.sh --check` every tick, sync on drift). The two timers' overlap is itself a box fact worth a look — the updater's tick walks later hourly (monotonic timer) and will cross sync-main's :01 slot periodically; nothing breaks now, but the restart interlock and the fast-forward are being decided by two units. | Measured 2026-09-21 on vigil: `journalctl -u tepna-sync-main` 10:01:18 "main fast-forwarded 6 commit(s): 7220e686 -> abbbf15d"; `journalctl -u tepna-update` 10:02:34 "up to date at abbbf15dd123 — nothing to do"; `sync-apps.sh --check` afterwards: "35 bundle(s): 5 current, 30 stale". Reflog: fast-forwards at 06:57 (update), 07:02 (sync-main), 07:59, 08:59 (update), 10:01 (sync-main). Fix: two tests — a stale served tree is synced when this tick moved nothing; a current one is not re-copied. | fixed #2761 |
| 2026-09-21-validaterr-compares-gap-spanning-intervals | 2026-09-21 | `MEASUREMENT-PROVENANCE-ROADMAP-2026-08-26-BRIEF.md` | **`ECGDex.validateRR` — and the export's `validation` block built on it — compares a self train that still contains the intervals straddling a dropout, so on any night with a gap the published cross-check is off by orders of magnitude.** `analyze` flags those intervals (`nnSpansGap`, §∅) and excludes them from the headline rMSSD/SDNN, but never publishes the mask and hands `validateRR`/`alignFirmwareRR` the raw `r.nn`. On 2026-09-03 the train carries 22 intervals > 3000 ms (largest 1 232 840 ms — a 20-minute dropout counted as one beat-to-beat interval); the shipped export reads `validation.dRMSSDPct = 65797.8` and `dSDNNPct = 17458.3` against a firmware rMSSD of 34.9 ms. With the firmware parser's own 200–3000 ms window applied to both trains the same night reads self 32.4 vs 34.9 ms, dRMSSD 7.1 %, dSDNN 5.6 %. A consumer reading `validation` as "does our detector agree with the strap" gets NO on every gappy night for a reason that has nothing to do with the detector. | 2026-09-22-mutation-summary-parsed-exit-code | 2026-09-22 | `.github/workflows/mutation.yml` | **The diff-scoped JS mutation summary rendered the gate's EXIT CODE, `0 → PASS`, and exit 0 is also what `mutate.mjs --diff` returns when it decided nothing** — when no mutable JS changed (pre-run exit), and when the changed lines carried no mutable operator (it printed `✓ mutation gate: all 0 mutant(s) … were killed`). Measured over the last 300 first-parent commits on main (2026-09-14 → 09-22), re-enumerating each commit's changed lines with the current `mutantsFor` under the gate's own `-w` diff: 264 took the pre-run exit and the gate RAN on 36, of which **9 had zero mutants on the changed lines** — so **273 of 300 commits read PASS on a gate that examined nothing on them**, and the nine checkmarks-over-nothing were invisible until `mutate.mjs` emitted `tepna.verdict/1` and the schema refused PASS over `checked = 0`. | 2026-09-22-cvhr-cross-node-comparability-unsupported | 2026-09-22 | `ppgdex-dsp.js` | **`ppgdex-dsp.js` holds `PPG_CVHR_GAP_S = 10; // ECGDex GAP_S — one cut, so the two nodes' indices stay comparable`, and on the 23 paired box nights the two CVHR indices are NOT comparable across nights at any cut.** The GAP_S sweep (owner: measure before ruling) swept both constants together over 3–60 s: neither index moves with the cut (ECG-SATURATION-ABSENCE §GAP_S item), so the cut is not what decides comparability — and at every value the ECGDex and PpgDex indices rank-correlate across nights at Spearman 0.03–0.05 with a median \|Δ\| of 1.0–1.1 ev/h (medians 6.2 vs 5.9). Not a defect in either detector by itself (different signals, different beat trains); a defect in what the comment claims and in anything downstream that treats the two indices as interchangeable. Remedy is a decision, not a fix: either the comment states only what the shared cut buys (definitional alignment), or a comparability claim is made and measured on nights where both nodes see the same events | `audits/GAP-S-SWEEP-2026-09-22.json` `report.perValue.<v>.cross` (nights 23, spearman 0.032…0.052, medianAbsDiff 1.0…1.1) for every v in 3,5,7.5,10,15,20,30,60; null control in `tools/gap-s-sweep.mjs --selftest` shows the instrument sees a planted 20 s gap | OPEN |

| 2026-09-22-capture-filename-stamp-disagrees-with-content | 2026-09-22 | `audits/GAP-S-SWEEP-2026-09-22.json` | **39 box capture files on 14 nights carry a filename stamp more than 5 minutes from their own first data row, and 8 of them are 13-19 HOURS out — enough to move the night they are grouped into.** Found by asking why two instruments disagreed about whether data exists: on 2026-08-24 and 2026-08-28 the fold (`trio-batch`) reported NO concurrent Verity session while the GAP_S sweep scored a Verity file on both. The sessions ARE concurrent with that night's ECG; the NAME says otherwise, so they are dropped and the night folds without a Verity leg. **Neither tool is wrong in isolation** — the fold trusting the name is the amplifier, the capture-host writing it is the source, and a reader comparing the two instruments would see a data-existence disagreement with no explanation. Census over 2,689 files > 5 kB: 39 disagree > 5 min (max 18.7 h), 14 nights, both streams of a session always together (so it is the session's start stamp, not a per-file slip). Capture-host lane: why the stamp is written from something other than the first sample (a scheduled/attempted start time?) is Heron's to read off the box; NOT touched here. Consumers that group by filename are the blast radius | `audits/GAP-S-SWEEP-2026-09-22.json` `foldRescore.missingVerityExplained` (the two nights, the census, every disagreeing file with its stamp, first row and delta); the fold's grouping in `tools/trio-batch.mjs` (scan → `nightKeyOf(t0)` from the parsed filename stamp, `concurrentSet`) **Committed-corpus impact, measured:** 8 of the 14 nights are already in `uploads/trio` (119 committed nights) — 2026-08-12/20/21/23/24/25/27 and 09-10 — and three of the four night-key-moving nights are committed (08-20, 08-21, 08-24). | 2026-09-22-startup-transient-refutation-underpowered | 2026-09-22 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **CORRECTS the REFUTATION half of `2026-09-21-capture-daemon-qc-digest-peaks-1-3gb`: its ten-minute window ends BEFORE the step it reports the absence of.** That row withdrew `2026-09-20-capture-daemon-startup-peak-1-3gb` as mis-named on two legs \u2014 (a) the 1.3 GB VmHWM coincides with the 09:00 QC digest, and (b) *"a 09:07 restart stayed at 92 MB for ten minutes (startup sampler: 83\u201386 MB, flat, no transient)"*. Leg (a) stands and is untouched here. **Leg (b) does not.** `tools/box-sample.mjs`'s own 2026-09-15 traces (259 samples, three cycles, rows `2026-09-15-capture-rss-peak-is-startup` / `-saturates`) put the step between **604 s and 665 s** of process age \u2014 peak **123 MB at 604 s**, **667 MB at 665 s** \u2014 saturating at ~760 MB by 1.70 h. Ten minutes is **600 s**, so the observation stops **4\u201365 s short of the step**, and its 92 MB at 600 s AGREES with the earlier curve at the same elapsed time rather than contradicting it. A window that short cannot tell *"no startup transient"* from *"the transient has not started yet"*, so no startup attribution is established EITHER WAY. \u26a0\ufe0f Two further reasons not to settle this from the existing numbers: the peaks differ (**760 MB** vs **1.30 GB**), so a saturating startup transient and a digest peak may BOTH exist and be different phenomena; and the 09-15 transient\u2019s suspected cause (device discovery / adapter setup, never tested) would not fire identically on a night with no ring data. | Arithmetic over committed rows only \u2014 no box access needed: 604 s / 665 s / 1.70 h from rows 235\u2013236, "ten minutes" and 92 MB from row 308, all three rows in this ledger. | 2026-09-22-a-hatch-that-leaves-no-trace | 2026-09-22 | `.claude/hooks/guard-shared-tree.sh` | **`CLAUDE_ALLOW_UNFORMATTED` disables TWO guards, and that is this row's thesis arriving unchosen.** A session exporting it for a deliberate WIP JS commit also silently turns off the capture-host **ruff** check it never considered. `guard-ruff.sh`'s header documents the sharing, so it was deliberate AT THE POINT OF WRITING and is invisible AT THE POINT OF USE — which is exactly the gap between a hatch and a hole, not an illustration of it. **A hatch's scope should be as narrow as the rule it bypasses**; one variable per guard is the obvious repair and costs a line each — named here as an OPTION, since whoever picks this up owns that call. &#x2014; **And the general form: a deliberate bypass leaves NO trace anywhere.** Every guard honours its hatch as an early `exit 0` before any rule, and nothing records that it was honoured: no log line, no stderr note, no artifact. A session that bypasses for a good reason and one that bypasses to get past an inconvenient denial are the same event to every later reader, including the session itself an hour on. That poses a POLICY question with three answers, costing differently: **silent** (today — zero cost, zero evidence), **noted** (one stderr line naming the bypassed rule — visible to the session and its transcript, invisible to anyone else, no storage), **recorded** (a line in a file a peer or a gate can read — real evidence, and a new artifact somebody must own and prune). Whoever picks this up should CHOOSE among the three rather than implement the first one named. Surfaced by #2871, whose denial paragraph asks the user to SELF-REPORT hatch use precisely because no mechanism can observe it. | Enumerated 2026-09-22 across `.claude/hooks/` on `origin/main`, independently by two sessions: **six** guards carry a hatch and **five** variables cover them — `guard-shared-tree.sh` (`CLAUDE_ALLOW_BLANKET_GIT`), `guard-stale-brief.sh` (`CLAUDE_ALLOW_STALE_BRIEF`), `guard-memory-stale.sh` (`CLAUDE_ALLOW_STALE_MEMORY`), `guard-doc-search.sh` (`CLAUDE_ALLOW_NO_DOC_SEARCH`), and **`guard-format.sh` + `guard-ruff.sh` sharing `CLAUDE_ALLOW_UNFORMATTED`** (`guard-format.sh:58`, and `guard-ruff.sh:45` documenting the sharing). Each is an early `exit 0` before any rule. &#x26A0; The count was stated as THREE when this was routed and measured as SIX; it is pinned by name here so the population is a set rather than a number. &#x26A0; Mechanical note for the picker-up, not a defect: `docs-ledger` check8b rejects a DIRECTORY as a row source, so the source cell names one hook file and the other five are named above. | OPEN |
| 2026-09-22-sentinel-waiter-outlives-its-producer | 2026-09-22 | `CLAUDE.md` | **CLAUDE.md §👥.4's option 3 — the `EXIT=` sentinel — cannot terminate when the PRODUCER is killed, and the resulting waiter is indistinguishable from a slow gate.** §4 exists to stop a waiter that never exits, and option 3 is the form people copy because it looks simplest. It protects against the CHILD dying (`( cmd ; echo "EXIT=$?" )` still writes the line) but **not against the subshell itself being SIGKILLed** — which is precisely §4c's scenario, where the harness watchdog reaps a background task under box memory pressure. The line is never written, so `until grep -q '^EXIT=' log` polls forever. §4 and §4c are each correct in isolation; the gap is that §4c's failure mode defeats §4's prescribed remedy and neither section says so. **The fix is small and already half-present in the same section:** option 2 (own the PID, `kill -0`) is immune and additionally yields the exit code, which `grep` structurally cannot — so option 3 needs either a PID check beside the sentinel or its own deadline, plus one line naming why. Do NOT delete option 3: a sentinel survives a waiter that starts *after* the producer, which owning a PID does not — option 2 is immune to this and not to that, so an editor who "simplifies" by keeping one form re-opens the other hole. **The two cases must stay distinguishable to a reader skimming for the snippet:** the PID guard applies when YOU launched the producer; a waiter in a DIFFERENT shell has no `$PID`, and there the sentinel alone is still the right form — with a deadline. ⚠️ This is the THIRD stale prescription in CLAUDE.md found on 2026-09-22 (with `queue-doctor` still advising auto-merge that Kodiak made meaningless, and `stale-root-is-the-default` prescribing `merge --ff-only origin/main` for a root not on main) — the count is the finding: a prescription in the fleet's instruction sheet that cannot run is worse than none, and three in one day says the file's advice is not re-tested against the tooling it describes. | Measured 2026-09-22, both legs, with a positive control. **The real instance:** two of this session's own waiters (`until grep -q '^EXIT=' refold3.log`) polled from the aborted corpus fold's death at 13:00:29 until they were stopped at 15:08:49 — **2 h 08 min** past their producer, and `grep -c '^EXIT=' refold3.log` returns **0** because the fold was killed before its sentinel. **Option 3, controlled:** `( sleep 60; echo "EXIT=$?" ) > s3.log &` then `kill -9` the subshell ⇒ log is **0 bytes, 0 sentinel lines**; a waiter on it never exits. **Option 2, identical kill:** `sleep 60 & PID=$!; kill -9 $PID; while kill -0 $PID; do …; done; wait $PID` ⇒ loop **exits after 1 poll** and reports **137** (SIGKILL). Same kill, opposite outcome, so the discriminator is the waiting form and not the kill. | OPEN |

Today the answer is reconstructed by hand from dates, which is exactly the reasoning the provenance machinery exists to replace. | 2026-09-22-wfdb-invalid-sample-not-recognised | 2026-09-22 | `tools/ecg-physionet-differential.mjs` | **`tools/ecg-physionet-differential.mjs` does not recognise WFDB's in-band invalid-sample sentinel, so an undefined sample enters the shipped ECG DSP as signal — §∅'s O2Ring failure with a different constant.** `wfdb.h` (wfdblib 10.7.0) defines `WFDB_INVALID_SAMPLE (-32768)`, the minimum 16-bit sample, returned by `getvec`/`getframe` when an amplitude is undefined; the standard's own comment concedes the in-band hazard (*"in a format 24 or 32 signal, this value will be near mid-range"*). `readDat`'s format-16 path (`:201`) is a bare `buf.readInt16LE` and nothing downstream distinguishes it, so `scoreRecord` scales it to physical µV — **−163.84 mV at a typical gain of 200 ADU/mV** — and hands it to `ECGDSP.analyze`. &#x26A0; **LIVE IN PRINCIPLE, UNMEASURED IN THE RECORDS WE HAVE SCORED**, and the fix owes that measurement as its DELIVERABLE rather than as a formality: the tool has published numbers, so if none move, that is a measurement of how rare the sentinel is in our corpus (and the answer to whether this is live at all); if they move, those numbers were computed over fabricated samples. &#x26A0; **THE MEASUREMENT DECIDES WHAT IS REPORTED, NOT WHETHER.** The defect is the INABILITY to distinguish a sentinel from signal — §∅'s own argument is that *a consumer cannot null what it cannot distinguish* — so it stands on the merits whether or not it happened to fire on the records scored so far. "Nothing moved, so there is nothing to say" is how a real gap goes unreported after being found and fixed, and §∅ is the rule the owner called absolute priority precisely because it had been true in two places for two thousand commits with nobody reporting it. The honest close if nothing moves is *"a §∅ gap in the PhysioNet reader, fixed, and it had not fired on any record we have scored"*. &#x26A0; **A SECOND HAZARD AIMED AT THE FIX, not at today's numbers:** the same file MANUFACTURES that constant at `:416` — `Math.max(-32768, …)` clamping scaled values into the buffer handed to `ECGDSP` — so within one file −32768 means "undefined" on the read path and "a real sample, saturated" on the other. Nulling it at the parse boundary without touching the clamp would null values the clamp produces on purpose. The clamp is **latent**: reaching it needs a physical amplitude ≤ −32.768 mV where real ECG is ±5 mV, and MIT-BIH's 11-bit range at gain 200 cannot reach it — a trap for whoever changes gain handling or adds a database, not a defect producing wrong numbers today. &#x26A0; **NOT CLAIMED:** whether WFDB maps the lowest expressible value of a sub-16-bit format (−2048 for format 212) to the same sentinel. The library NEWS mentions such a mapping being repaired for formats 80 and 160, which makes it plausible and **unverified**; an unverified equivalence is what `docs/WFDB-INTEROP-MAPPING.md` §6 refuses to write down. | Read from the primary source 2026-09-22, not from recollection: `physionet.org/physiotools/wfdb/lib/wfdb.h` (wfdblib 10.7.0) — `#define WFDB_INVALID_SAMPLE (-32768)` with the quoted comment; behaviour documented in the WFDB Programmer's Guide §2.2 "Special I/O Modes". &#x26A0; **`SIGNAL(5)` does NOT mention it** — it is a library constant, not a file-format one, so an adapter author reading only the format spec never meets it. Code read on `origin/main`: `tools/ecg-physionet-differential.mjs:201` (format-16 read, no guard), `:416` (the clamp), `:422` (the buffer reaching `ECGDSP.analyze`). The buffer is **never serialised** — the only writers in that file (`pack212`, the annotation packer) build selftest fixtures — so there is no WFDB artifact carrying a fabricated absence; an earlier reading of this row's second half as a "write path" was traced to the consumer and withdrawn before filing. | fixed #2879 |
| 2026-09-22-residue-dirty-carries-no-signal | 2026-09-22 | `briefs/RESIDUE.md` | **GitHub marks every `RESIDUE.md`-touching PR `DIRTY` once `main` moves, and that status has ZERO DISCRIMINATING POWER.** The ledger is append-only behind a `merge=residue` union driver; GitHub computes mergeability **without** custom drivers, so rows added on both sides read as a conflict the driver resolves silently. **A genuinely conflicted PR and a benign one present IDENTICALLY**, so the state has to be re-diagnosed before it can be dismissed \u2014 a status that reads the same whether or not the thing is wrong is not evidence. Four instances on 2026-09-22 alone (#2871, #2877, #2878, #2880); since a row is filed on most units this is now the DEFAULT for such a PR, not an occasional surprise. \u26a0\ufe0f **And the remedy is NOT "know it is benign": the two cases really do differ**, just not in the field GitHub shows, so treating every DIRTY as benign would wave a real conflict through. | **The discriminator is one line and it is the remedy:** `git merge-tree $(git merge-base origin/main origin/<branch>) origin/<branch> origin/main \| grep -c '^<<<<<<<'` \u2014 **0 \u21d2 benign** (`git merge origin/main`, then a plain push; never rebase, which replays rows against a moving ledger, and never force), **non-zero \u21d2 real**, resolve by hand. Opposite verdicts the same day: **0** on `claude/root-reads-tests-and-equivalences-osp`, markers in three capture-host files on `claude/daemon-start-observability-wrn`. \u26a0\ufe0f **Then verify by CONTENT, because a silent driver failure resolves cleanly and looks identical to success:** count rows on both sides and check the arithmetic closes \u2014 280 on `main` + 3 added = **283** on the branch, each new key grepped back. A clean tree is not the check; the row count is. | OPEN |

| 2026-09-22-fresh-worktree-silences-both-format-checks | 2026-09-22 | `.claude/hooks/guard-format.sh` | **A fresh worktree is the ONE environment where BOTH the local format check and the commit hook are silent — and CLAUDE.md §👥.1 mandates worktrees, so the fleet spends most of its time there.** `git worktree add` does not populate `node_modules`, so `./node_modules/.bin/biome` exits **127** (missing binary). The second layer does not catch it either: `guard-format.sh` **fails open** where Biome cannot run, deliberately and correctly, *because* a fresh worktree has no `node_modules`. So the two mechanisms have the same blind spot rather than covering each other, and the file reaches CI unformatted. ⚠️ **The cost is not just a CI lap.** Measured on #2882: unformatted source was reformatted by CI's Biome, the reformat **wrapped an array across 14 lines**, and a source-scanning gate anchored on a single-line literal then located nothing — surfacing as **six flag-forwarding failures** that looked like six broken features and were one blind gate. So the 127 turns into a red that does not name formatting anywhere in its output. **Remedy is not to make the hook fail closed** (it would then deny every commit in a fresh worktree, which is the mandated workflow): the cheap fixes are a worktree bootstrap that links or installs `node_modules`, or a check that distinguishes *absent* from *clean* and says so. | 2026-09-22-heap-retry-would-misdiagnose-enospc | 2026-09-22 | `tools/trio-batch.mjs` | **The heap retry added in #2882 makes a full disk HARDER to diagnose, not easier — a hazard created by that change and recorded at creation time rather than found later.** `isHeapOom` accepts a bare exit **134** with no heap message, deliberately (a wasted retry costs one run of one night; a missed heap death costs the night). A child that dies of **ENOSPC** can abort the same way, so the parent would classify it as a cap problem and **retry it at 4096 MB — the wrong remedy, applied confidently, to a cause the retry cannot touch.** The night then fails twice and the run reports a heap narrative for a disk fault. The exposure is real rather than theoretical on this box: `/` stands at **100 % (1.2 G free of 158 G)** with 58 worktrees and several sessions writing to it, while the fold's OWN appetite is negligible — it rewrites `uploads/trio` in place, 401 MB total, largest single export **2.2 MB**, `writeFileSync` with no temp+rename, and **zero** `/tmp` staging. So an ENOSPC inside a fold would most likely be caused by another session's write: a box-wide cause landing inside a 78-minute run. ⚠️ **NOT FIXED HERE, deliberately.** Widening `isHeapOom` to exclude ENOSPC on a single hypothetical is promotion-without-evidence, and narrowing it re-opens the missed-heap-death case it was widened for. The partial mitigation already ships: a second failure at `RETRY_HEAP_MB` prints `this is not a cap problem`, which is a breadcrumb rather than a diagnosis. The unit, if an instance ever occurs, is to read the child's stderr for `ENOSPC`/`no space left` BEFORE the 134 branch — an ordering change, not a new signal — and it wants one real occurrence first. | Measured 2026-09-22: `df -h /` 1.2 G free / 158 G / 100 %; `/srv/data` (inputs, read-only) 58 G free; `/tmp` 29 G free and unused by the fold (`grep -cE 'tmpdir\|os\.tmpdir\|/tmp' tools/trio-batch.mjs` = 0); `du -sh uploads/trio` 401 MB over 140 nights, mean 2.9 MB/night, largest export 2.2 MB; `grep -c renameSync` = 0. The retry and the `isHeapOom` trade are in `tools/trio-batch.mjs` (#2882), whose selftest names the accepted case: *a bare 134 with no heap message IS retried — a wasted retry beats a missed night*. | OPEN |
